# Research Frontier: Spin(2,3), Division Algebras, the Standard Model, and Stress–Energy

**Status:** canonical synthesis and research-frontier document  
**Date:** 19 October 2026  
**Scope:** synthesis of Aaron's Spin(2,3) thesis, the Standard Model stress–energy analysis, the review of Cohl Furey's programme, and the complete 525-file historical corpus audit  
**Authority:** this document distinguishes established physics, exact model-local results, conditional constructions, analogies, and open conjectures. It is not a claim that a completed unified theory has been obtained.

**Research grammar:** the claim labels below do not reduce the frontier to a binary true/false list. Live possibilities should be represented as branches—including uncomfortable branches—with explicit discriminants, dependencies, and stop-everything triggers. See the [open-branch research method](32_open_branch_research_method.md).

---

## Abstract

The research programme asks whether the observed Lorentzian spacetime sector and the internal representation content of the Standard Model can arise as compatible selections from a larger algebraic structure. Its most defensible present form combines three ingredients:

1. a constrained (Spin(2,3))-related geometric sector that reduces to (Spin(1,3)) gravity;
2. a division-algebraic internal sector, informed especially by Cohl Furey's work, that organises Standard Model fermions, charges, chirality, generations, and candidate Higgs structures;
3. one covariant action whose metric response is the total stress–energy tensor and whose internal variations produce the gauge currents.

The corpus contains exact algebraic identities, reproducible reduced-model calculations, topological model results, and a mature adversarial research framework. It does **not** yet contain a unique ambient parent, a complete physical derivation of the Standard Model, a demonstrated ghost-free literal two-time theory, a derived Yukawa sector, or a new experimentally confirmed prediction. Later work in the corpus correctly exposes this non-identifiability and preserves null models.

The frontier is therefore no longer “find another suggestive group chain.” It is to construct and audit the smallest common action that simultaneously supplies the physical state space, anomaly cancellation, geometric reduction, positive spectrum, Standard Model limit, total stress–energy, and one branch-discriminating observable.

---

## 1. The frontier in one sentence

> A constrained algebra of geometric and division-algebraic actions may possess a physical ideal whose stabiliser is \(Spin(1,3)\times G_{SM}\), whose low-energy dynamics are the Standard Model coupled to gravity, and whose selector sector leaves a small, correlated, falsifiable residual.

This is a research conjecture, not a result. It is narrower than the early corpus narrative and stronger scientifically because it states the required objects, separates external geometry from internal algebra, and admits clear failure conditions.

The programme's decisive deliverable is not another embedding or dimensional coincidence. It is:

> **a ghost-free action, a correct physical state table, and a prediction.**

---

## 2. How to read the programme

### 2.1 Claim types

Every important statement should carry one of the following statuses:

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `STANDARD` | Established external mathematics or physics with an appropriate source |
| `EXACT-LOCAL` | Proved or computed inside a fully declared model in this corpus |
| `CONDITIONAL` | Follows if named postulates, choices, or ansätze hold |
| `CANDIDATE-BRIDGE` | A proposed map between structures whose physical validity is still open |
| `ANALOGY` | A useful resemblance that is not evidence of common microscopic origin |
| `EMPIRICAL` | Connected to data through an operational map and uncertainty model |
| `NO-GO` | Excludes a stated class of models under stated assumptions |
| `SUPERSEDED` | Replaced by a later correction or stronger analysis |
| `RETRACT` | False or materially misleading as originally written |

The central discipline is:

\[
\text{same dimension or visual pattern}
\not\Rightarrow \text{canonical map}
\not\Rightarrow \text{physical identity}
\not\Rightarrow \text{dynamical explanation}.
\]

Each arrow requires its own proof.

### 2.2 Authority hierarchy

When documents disagree, the preferred order is:

1. externally established mathematics and measured physics;
2. exact calculations with explicit assumptions and reproducible code;
3. later governed research rounds and registered negative results;
4. declared paper-local models;
5. current synthesis documents;
6. early compendia, conversations, and speculative atlases.

An exact theorem in a toy Hamiltonian remains exact for that Hamiltonian. It does not become evidence that the Hamiltonian is fundamental. Conversely, failure of the parent theory does not invalidate a correct paper-local topological or algebraic result.

### 2.3 Discovery and warrant

The older-LLM-assisted corpus was strong at discovery: it found analogies, candidate algebras, and possible links at unusual breadth. It was weak at warrant: it often moved too quickly from resemblance to ontology. The later corpus is strongest where it converts those ideas into typed maps, independent checks, null hypotheses, branch matrices, and no-go results.

The correct editorial operation is therefore **salvage, type, and test**, not wholesale deletion and not uncritical accumulation.

---

## 3. The established baseline

Any proposed synthesis must reproduce this baseline before it can modify it.

### 3.1 Spacetime and gravity

Observed local relativistic physics is organised by Lorentz symmetry (Spin(1,3)), ordinarily on a four-dimensional manifold with one causal time. General relativity couples geometry to the variational response of the full matter action:

\[
T_{\mu\nu}
=-\frac{2}{\sqrt{-g}}\frac{\delta S_{\mathrm{matter}}}{\delta g^{\mu\nu}}.
\]

The Bianchi identity and diffeomorphism invariance imply covariant conservation of the **total** stress tensor on shell:

\[
\nabla^\mu T^{\mathrm{total}}_{\mu\nu}=0.
\]

This is the precise sense in which gravity responds to matter. Stress–energy is not an inventory of particle names; it is the metric response of a dynamical action.

### 3.2 The Standard Model

Locally, the Standard Model gauge algebra is

\[
\mathfrak g_{SM}=\mathfrak{su}(3)_c\oplus\mathfrak{su}(2)_L\oplus\mathfrak u(1)_Y.
\]

The faithful global gauge group may involve a discrete quotient, conventionally expressed as

\[
G_{SM}\simeq
\frac{SU(3)_c\times SU(2)_L\times U(1)_Y}{\mathbb Z_6},
\]

with global form depending on the precise matter content and formulation.

Per generation, the chiral multiplets are

\[
Q_L:(\mathbf3,\mathbf2)_{1/6},\quad
u_R:(\mathbf3,\mathbf1)_{2/3},\quad
d_R:(\mathbf3,\mathbf1)_{-1/3},
\]

\[
L_L:(\mathbf1,\mathbf2)_{-1/2},\quad
e_R:(\mathbf1,\mathbf1)_{-1},
\]

with the neutrino sector depending on whether right-handed neutrinos and Majorana masses are added. The Higgs transforms as

\[
H:(\mathbf1,\mathbf2)_{1/2}.
\]

The theory includes Yang–Mills terms, chiral fermion kinetic terms, the Higgs kinetic term and potential, and Yukawa couplings. A unification proposal must recover the representations, chirality, anomaly cancellation, Higgs quantum numbers, mass-generating invariants, and the global gauge-group action—not just the Lie-algebra dimensions.

### 3.3 Standard Model stress–energy

For a generally covariant Standard Model action, the classical Hilbert tensor can be organised as

\[
T^{SM}_{\mu\nu}
=T^{gauge}_{\mu\nu}
+T^{H}_{\mu\nu}
+T^{fermion}_{\mu\nu}
+T^{interaction}_{\mu\nu}
+T^{improvement}_{\mu\nu}.
\]

Schematically, with (X_{\mu\nu}\in\{G^A_{\mu\nu},W^I_{\mu\nu},B_{\mu\nu}\}),

\[
T^{gauge}_{\mu\nu}
=\sum_X\left(
X_{\mu\alpha}X_\nu{}^\alpha
-\frac14g_{\mu\nu}X_{\alpha\beta}X^{\alpha\beta}
\right),
\]

\[
T^H_{\mu\nu}
=(D_\mu H)^\dagger D_\nu H
+(D_\nu H)^\dagger D_\mu H
-g_{\mu\nu}\left[(D_\alpha H)^\dagger D^\alpha H+V(H)\right],
\]

and the symmetric fermion term is

\[
T^{fermion}_{\mu\nu}
=\frac{i}{4}\sum_f
\bar f\left(
\gamma_\mu\overleftrightarrow D_\nu
+\gamma_\nu\overleftrightarrow D_\mu
\right)f
-g_{\mu\nu}\mathcal L_f,
\]

with interaction and potential contributions included consistently through the full metric variation. A nonminimal term such as (-\xi R H^\dagger H) adds the corresponding curvature/improvement contribution. Gauge fixing and Faddeev–Popov ghosts enter off-shell quantum identities but do not represent asymptotic physical matter.

After renormalisation, (T_{\mu\nu}) is a composite operator. It can mix with allowed local curvature operators, has trace anomalies, and must satisfy the appropriate Ward identities. Any claim that “the Standard Model is a stress tensor” should therefore mean:

> the Standard Model fields and interactions define a covariantly conserved metric-response operator within a complete generally covariant action.

It must not mean that stress–energy alone reconstructs gauge representations, couplings, flavour, or quantum state content.

### 3.4 Why stress–energy is the right interface

Stress–energy is the natural meeting point of the programme because it is:

- universal at the level of gravitational response;
- derived from the same action as the field equations;
- sensitive to hidden or selector sectors through their energy, momentum, pressure, and anisotropic stress;
- constrained by Ward identities and conservation;
- measurable indirectly through gravitational and inertial effects.

But it is an interface, not a completed unifier. Different quantum field theories can possess stress tensors with the same macroscopic form. The internal representation problem remains separate.

---

## 4. The Spin(2,3) geometric frontier

### 4.1 What the group supplies

(Spin(2,3)) is the double cover of (SO(2,3)) and is isomorphic to (Sp(4,\mathbb R)). It naturally appears as the isometry group associated with anti-de Sitter four-geometry and as a possible Cartan gauge group whose breaking can leave (Spin(1,3)).

These are established mathematical facts. They do not by themselves imply that nature has two physical times.

### 4.2 Three ontologies that must not be conflated

#### A. Cartan-geometric ontology — preferred frontier

Work on ordinary four-dimensional spacetime with a (Spin(2,3)) connection

\[
\mathcal A_\mu{}^{AB}
\]

and a compensator or selector (V^A) of fixed norm. A vacuum value of (V^A) leaves a (Spin(1,3)) stabiliser. The connection then decomposes into a Lorentz spin connection and tetrad-like broken components.

This option uses the larger group without adding a second observable clock. It offers the cleanest route to Einstein–Cartan or MacDowell–Mansouri-type gravity and should be the default ontology.

#### B. Configuration-space ontology

The additional timelike-sign coordinate belongs to a field space, internal configuration manifold, or sigma-model target rather than physical spacetime. Its gradients and potential can contribute to four-dimensional stress–energy. This remains viable only if the fluctuation metric and physical spectrum are positive after constraints and gauge reduction.

#### C. Literal two-time spacetime — quarantined until proved consistent

A spacetime with two real timelike coordinates generically faces ultrahyperbolicity, negative-norm modes, unstable Hamiltonians, and an ill-posed ordinary initial-value problem. A viable version requires an explicit first-class constraint system or gauge principle whose physical quotient has a single causal time.

Free Fourier momentum along a second real time does not automatically generate ordinary mass. In the simplest mostly-plus scalar convention it contributes with the wrong sign:

\[
m_{eff}^2=-\omega_2^2.
\]

Therefore the early “hidden temporal momentum becomes mass” argument is superseded unless a different constrained mechanism is supplied.

### 4.3 The selector problem

The central geometric question is not merely how (Spin(1,3)) embeds in (Spin(2,3)), but why one Lorentzian sector is physically selected and dynamically preserved.

The selector must answer:

1. What is the order parameter or compensator?
2. What fixes its norm and orientation?
3. What is its residual gauge group?
4. What are its propagating modes?
5. Does it carry positive energy?
6. How does it couple to matter?
7. Does its evolution preserve the selected fermion module?
8. What observable remains after the heavy modes decouple?

Later corpus rounds produce exact selector spectra for declared reduced models, but the physical origin of the selector and its unique parent remain open.

### 4.4 Corrected early intuitions

- A null vector in (mathbb R^{2,3}) does not by itself yield a nondegenerate (3+1) Lorentzian subspace; its orthogonal complement is degenerate.
- Returning to an initial temporal orientation does not imply a nontrivial winding; a path may retrace unless topology or dynamics forbids it.
- Compact subgroup geometry and Hopf fibrations can organise phase and representation structure but do not by themselves derive fermion statistics, mass, or magnetic moments.
- A coordinate tilt in a flat higher-dimensional space does not create missing gravitating matter. Physical effects require fields, curvature, boundary conditions, or modified couplings.
- A shift vector can be a coordinate artefact. “Warp” requires invariant global geometry, sources, and causal analysis.

These corrections narrow the programme to structures that can be varied, quantised, and tested.

---

## 5. The Furey internal-algebra frontier

### 5.1 What the programme genuinely achieves

Cohl Furey's work develops a coherent representation programme based on the real normed division algebras

\[
\mathbb R,\quad\mathbb C,\quad\mathbb H,\quad\mathbb O
\]

and their complexifications, multiplication algebras, Clifford actions, minimal ideals, and symmetry intersections. The programme demonstrates that structures resembling Standard Model multiplets and charges can arise naturally from algebraic ideals rather than being entered only as an external list.

The strongest established or construction-level achievements include:

- complex quaternionic structures supporting Lorentz-spinor behaviour;
- complex octonionic chains generating a Clifford algebra action;
- Witt decompositions and fermionic ladder operators;
- minimal left ideals with states carrying one-generation-like colour and electric-charge assignments;
- a number operator whose eigenvalues reproduce the familiar charge pattern in the chosen ideal;
- symmetry stabilisers related to (SU(3)_c\times U(1)_Q);
- intersection methods that can select Standard-Model-like gauge algebras;
- multiple algebraic organisations of three generations;
- triality-based organisation of fermionic and scalar sectors;
- graded Jordan and nested-embedding constructions that make the programme more systematic.

This is important progress on the question: **why these representations?**

### 5.2 What is not yet derived

The algebraic construction does not yet supply, by itself:

- a complete spacetime-local quantum field theory;
- a unique dynamical action;
- a demonstrated physical origin of the chosen primitive idempotent or complex structure;
- the full chiral state table with every global identification independently audited;
- the observed Yukawa matrices, flavour mixing, and mass hierarchy;
- a quantum anomaly and BRST analysis of the final physical module;
- a unique Higgs potential and vacuum;
- a measured prediction beyond the Standard Model.

An algebraic stabiliser is not automatically a gauge symmetry of a local action. A representation copy is not automatically a physical generation. A scalar triality slot is not automatically the observed Higgs. These identifications require dynamics and operational consequences.

### 5.3 Competing generation mechanisms

The corpus contains at least three possible generation organisations:

1. multiple generation subspaces within a Clifford-chain construction;
2. triality slots such as ((\Psi_+,\Psi_-,V));
3. graded sectors in a larger Jordan/endomorphic structure such as (H_{16}(\mathbb C)).

The frontier calculation is an explicit intertwiner comparison. Either construct isomorphisms preserving charges, chirality, weak action, conjugation, and invariant forms, or show that the mechanisms are inequivalent. Until that is done, their multiplicities must not be added together or treated as independent evidence for “three.”

### 5.4 The correct relationship to Spin(2,3)

The safe architecture is factorised but coupled:

\[
\text{external geometric spin bundle}
\quad\otimes\quad
\text{internal division-algebraic module}.
\]

Spin(2,3) addresses selection of the effective Lorentzian geometric sector. Furey's algebra addresses selection of internal particle representations. They meet through one Dirac operator, one action, and one stress tensor.

The following proposed identifications are presently invalid:

- octonionic non-associativity (=) irreversible time;
- an internal imaginary unit (=) an extra physical timelike coordinate;
- Clifford signature (=) the number of experienced spacetime dimensions;
- triality (=) a three-strand temporal braid.

Each can remain an analogy or prompt, but none is a derivation.

## 6. The combined architecture

### 6.1 Kinematic data

Begin on a four-dimensional manifold (M) with:

- a principal (Spin(2,3)) bundle and connection (mathcal A^{AB});
- a selector (V^A) whose stabiliser is (Spin(1,3));
- an induced tetrad (e^a{}_{\mu}) and spin connection (omega^{ab}{}_{\mu});
- an internal space (mathcal F) generated from division-algebraic multiplication maps;
- internal projectors or compatible structures (P_F,J_a);
- a physical module (mathcal F_{phys}=P_F\mathcal F);
- matter fields

\[
\Psi\in\Gamma(S_V\otimes\mathcal F_{phys}),
\qquad
H\in\Gamma(E_H).
\]

Here (S_V) is the Lorentz spin bundle after geometric selection and (E_H) is the candidate Higgs representation.

### 6.2 Minimal action scaffold

The smallest useful action has the form

\[
S=S_{geom}+S_{selector}+S_{YM}+S_H+S_\Psi+S_Y+S_{portal}.
\]

A Cartan-geometric term may begin with

\[
S_{geom}=\alpha\int_M
\epsilon_{ABCDE}V^E\,\mathcal F^{AB}\wedge\mathcal F^{CD},
\]

supplemented by a selector constraint or potential. The low-energy limit must reproduce Einstein–Hilbert gravity, with any cosmological and topological terms explicitly identified.

The matter covariant derivative must combine the selected spin connection and internal gauge connection:

\[
\mathbb D_\mu
=\partial_\mu+\frac14\omega_\mu^{ab}\gamma_{ab}
-ig_sG_\mu^AT^A-igW_\mu^I\tau^I-ig'B_\mu Y
+\mathcal K_\mu,
\]

where (mathcal K_\mu) contains any selector or portal connection not already absorbed into the geometric decomposition.

The physical subspace must be dynamically preserved:

\[
(1-P)\slashed{\mathbb D}P=0,
\qquad
P=P_V\otimes P_F,
\]

unless the nonzero term is explicitly identified as a controlled mass, mixing, or portal operator. Otherwise a state selected as physical immediately evolves outside the proposed particle space.

### 6.3 Three response equations

The same action must generate:

\[
\frac{\delta S}{\delta\mathcal A_{geom}}
\quad\text{(geometric, torsional, and selector equations)},
\]

\[
\frac{\delta S}{\delta A_{int}}
\quad\text{(colour, weak, and hypercharge currents)},
\]

and

\[
-\frac{2}{\sqrt{-g}}\frac{\delta S}{\delta g^{\mu\nu}}
\quad\text{(total stress–energy)}.
\]

This forbids an important older-corpus failure mode: choosing one arbitrary kernel for what is electromagnetically visible and another unrelated kernel for what gravitates.

### 6.4 Total stress–energy and exchange

After reduction,

\[
T^{total}_{\mu\nu}
=T^{SM}_{\mu\nu}
+T^V_{\mu\nu}
+T^{portal}_{\mu\nu}
+T^{higher}_{\mu\nu}.
\]

If energy passes between visible and selector sectors, neither sector must be separately conserved:

\[
\nabla^\mu T^{SM}_{\mu\nu}=J_\nu,
\qquad
\nabla^\mu\left(T^V_{\mu\nu}+T^{portal}_{\mu\nu}\right)=-J_\nu.
\]

Only the total is conserved. This is the calculable form of the intuition that gravity may respond to more than a particular detector channel.

## 7. Present corpus result

### 7.1 What was reviewed

The historical physics corpus contains 525 files: prose, source code, data, generated relations, figures, manuscripts, and rendered papers. Its largest branch is the Spin(2,3) programme, accompanied by E8, exceptional Jordan, SO(2,4), Spin(3,3), Furey, Weinstein–Gu, Navier–Stokes/Jordan, symmetry-atlas, phonon, experiment, and speculative branches.

The software-level audit found:

- all 85 Python source files parse successfully;
- all 68 JSON files parse successfully;
- all 19 JavaScript/MJS files pass syntax checking;
- the existing corpus checker passes its overclaim and unqualified-Efimov checks;
- five documentation/path defects remain: two machine-specific paths and three broken local references.

This technical health is useful, but syntactic validity is not scientific validity.

### 7.2 Historical development

The corpus has three broad layers:

1. **Generative synthesis:** expansive claims connecting time, spin, topology, temperature, dark matter, warp, exceptional algebras, and the Standard Model.
2. **Modularisation:** smaller kernels, paper-local Hamiltonians, relation files, numerical checks, and explicit branch documents.
3. **Governed adversarial programme:** structured research packs, null models, admission criteria, parent tournaments, no-go outcomes, and explicit separation between discovery and warrant.

The third layer substantially improves the scientific quality of the project. It should govern the interpretation of the first two.

### 7.3 Strongest results

The strongest material consists of:

- exact calculations inside declared reduced models;
- reproducible selector spectra and finite-dimensional algebra;
- topological invariants of explicit Hamiltonians;
- explicit branch/world matrices;
- negative results showing that current data do not select a unique parent;
- a retained null baseline;
- Furey crosswalks that distinguish algebraic equality from physical origin;
- paper-level scope statements that limit claims to the constructed model;
- phonon and audio experiments used as analogue-method testbeds.

### 7.4 Current governed outcome

The later research rounds establish the following programme-level picture:

- no unique parent winner has been admitted;
- multiple reduced “worlds” remain live;
- SO(2,4) and Spin(3,3) remain exact-lift frontiers rather than established physical parents;
- selector spectra can be exact within chosen models, but selector origin remains open;
- spontaneous symmetry breaking has not been admitted as a universal solution;
- Furey-inspired internal structure is admitted at a constructional/algebraic level, not as a demonstrated physical origin;
- no controlled bridge from Geometric Unity has been established;
- no new empirical observable has yet been admitted;
- the null model remains viable.

This non-identification is a result, not a failure of the research method. It prevents premature convergence.

## 8. Status of candidate parent and bridge branches

| Branch | Current value | Current limit | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| (Spin(2,3)) | compact reduced laboratory; natural Cartan-gravity setting | selector origin and physical residual open | retain as central reduced programme |
| (SO(2,4)) | conformal geometry and exact structural relations | no admitted unique descent to the observed sector | retain in parent tournament |
| (Spin(3,3)) | useful Clifford and split-signature comparison | literal signature physics not established | retain as exact-lift frontier |
| E8 | rich representation context and exceptional-algebra links | proposed chain mixes subgroup, representation, automorphism, and analogy | retype every arrow before reuse |
| (J_3(\mathbb O)) | exceptional Jordan structure and (E_6/F_4) relations | observable-sector and dynamics bridge missing | retain as internal/parent candidate |
| Furey division algebras | strong Standard-Model representation organisation | dynamics, selector origin, parameters, and experiment missing | central internal branch |
| Weinstein–Gu | prompts geometric comparison and bridge questions | no controlled downstream common quantity admitted | research intake only |
| Navier–Stokes/Jordan | possible mathematical lemmas and coordinate embeddings | analytic gaps; embedding is not PDE regularity; handoffs overclaim | quarantine pending independent proof |

The early expression

\[
E_8\to E_6\to J_3(\mathbb O)\to G_2\to SO(2,4)\to Spin(2,3)
\]

must not be read as a uniform reduction chain. Each arrow has a different possible type and must be labelled as subgroup inclusion, representation, automorphism, stabiliser, projection, or conjectural bridge.

Dimension matches such as (15), (16), (27), (120), or (225) are search clues only. A physical identification additionally requires compatible multiplication, involution, representation action, dynamics, and observables.

## 9. Supporting corpora and laboratory pathway

### 9.1 Symmetry atlas

The symmetry corpus is useful as a conceptual index, but its oldest monolithic text blends textbook material with aggressive interpretive claims. Statements such as “everything dynamic is bosonic,” “temperature is a boson,” “the Bohr model is vindicated,” or “spin requires quaternions” should not be treated as admitted results.

Its best future role is as a sourced atlas that links each concept to a governed claim, calculation, or external reference.

### 9.2 Phonons and structured media

The phonon corpus is a comparatively grounded area. It develops lattice dynamics, normal modes, continuum limits, thermodynamics, and possible analogue structures. It is valuable because it can test the programme's methodology:

- controlled spectra;
- known microscopic equations;
- selectable geometries;
- measurable transfer functions;
- explicit noise and uncertainty.

Phonons can emulate aspects of effective geometry, topology, dissipation, and sector-dependent response. They do not demonstrate that spacetime or Standard Model particles are literally phononic.

### 9.3 Audio and response experiments

The browser and analysis experiments can prototype branch discrimination, preregistered observables, calibration, and null comparisons. Their scientific role is methodological unless a separately derived map connects the measured quantity to a fundamental parameter.

An analogue experiment should always report:

1. the exact analogue dictionary;
2. which equations are shared;
3. which symmetries are shared;
4. which microscopic ontology is **not** shared;
5. what failure teaches about the mathematical model;
6. why success does not automatically validate the fundamental interpretation.

### 9.4 Current papers

- **Two-branch transport paper:** a conditional nonassociative or branch-sensitive transport model; not a bulk derivation of fundamental physics.
- **DIII winding paper:** a topological result for a chosen Hamiltonian; physical authority requires derivation of that Hamiltonian and observable boundary response.
- **Dissipative Chern-plateau paper:** a conventional open-system/topological-matter construction; potentially publishable within its local scope.
- **Selector-spectrum material:** suitable for a mathematical methods or exact reduced-model note.
- **Parent tournament:** potentially valuable as a methodology or negative-result paper.

No paper-local result should be promoted into evidence for the complete synthesis without a typed physical bridge.

## 10. Consistency gates

A candidate synthesis must pass all of these gates before cosmological, dark-matter, or warp claims are entertained.

### Gate 1: representation completeness

Produce a machine-checkable table for every proposed physical state:

\[
(SU(3)_c,SU(2)_L,Y),\quad Q,\quad
\text{chirality},\quad\text{family},\quad
\text{carrier},\quad\text{projector}.
\]

Requirements:

- exactly the intended chiral multiplets;
- three generations without double counting;
- correct conjugations and reality conditions;
- a Higgs doublet of hypercharge (1/2);
- a declared neutrino sector;
- a faithful global gauge-group action.

### Gate 2: anomaly cancellation

On the actual selected module, compute:

\[
[SU(3)]^3,quad [SU(3)]^2U(1),quad
[SU(2)]^2U(1),quad U(1)^3,quad
\mathrm{gravity}^2U(1),
\]

as well as the global (SU(2)) anomaly. Algebraic resemblance to Standard Model multiplets is insufficient.

### Gate 3: dynamical preservation

The combined Dirac operator must preserve the selected state space or produce explicitly controlled transitions. Projector compatibility, Hermiticity, spin statistics, and gauge covariance must be verified.

### Gate 4: positivity and causal well-posedness

The linearised theory must have:

- positive residues at physical propagator poles;
- a Hamiltonian bounded below on the physical quotient;
- hyperbolic reduced equations;
- a well-posed initial-value problem;
- no observable second-clock mode;
- no accessible superluminal characteristics;
- a closed first-class constraint algebra where required.

### Gate 5: symmetry and Ward identities

Gauge, local Lorentz, diffeomorphism, selector, and BRST identities must be mutually compatible. Gauge fixing must not create or conceal physical negative-norm states.

### Gate 6: exact Standard Model and gravity limit

Below a scale (M_*), the effective action must take the form

\[
\mathcal L_{eff}=\mathcal L_{SM}+\mathcal L_{GR}
+\sum_{d>4}\frac{c_i^{(d)}}{M_*^{d-4}}\mathcal O_i^{(d)}.
\]

Every new effect must become a declared operator, state, threshold correction, or controlled nonlocal term—not an informal projection rule.

### Gate 7: empirical identifiability

At least one observable must:

- distinguish two or more live branches, including the null;
- be invariant under basis and convention choices;
- use the same parameter in laboratory and geometric response;
- include existing constraints and an uncertainty budget;
- have a preregistered failure condition.

## 11. The minimum decisive calculations

### 11.1 Calculation A — physical module and anomaly audit

Implement the proposed division-algebraic carrier, involutions, ladder operators, idempotents, and projectors in exact or symbolic arithmetic. Generate the complete state table and anomaly sums automatically.

**Decision:** failure ends the current internal construction; success admits it only to the next gate.

### 11.2 Calculation B — comparison of generation mechanisms

Construct explicit intertwiners among Clifford-chain, triality, and graded-Jordan generation spaces. Test preservation of charge, chirality, weak action, conjugation, and invariant bilinear/trilinear forms.

**Decision:** establish equivalence, partial overlap, or inequivalence. Do not accept dimension alone.

### 11.3 Calculation C — minimal selector vacuum and spectrum

Choose the smallest (Spin(2,3))-breaking action. Derive its vacuum, residual gauge symmetry, Hessian, constraints, pole masses, kinetic signs, and selector stress tensor.

**Decision:** any surviving physical ghost or ill-posed mode rejects the action.

### 11.4 Calculation D — compatible Dirac operator

Build (slashed{\mathbb D}) on (S_V\otimes\mathcal F) and compute

\[
(1-P)\slashed{\mathbb D}P.
\]

Classify every nonzero block as a gauge interaction, Yukawa/mass map, physical portal, or inconsistency.

### 11.5 Calculation E — Higgs and Yukawa invariant count

Compute every invariant trilinear

\[
\mathcal Y:\mathcal F_L\otimes E_H\otimes\mathcal F_R\to\mathbb C.
\]

Determine whether the algebra predicts texture zeros, coupling relations, scalar multiplicities, or unwanted flavour-changing operators. Free coefficients must remain honestly free if the algebra does not determine them.

### 11.6 Calculation F — full metric variation

Vary the complete reduced action, including selector and portal terms. Verify covariant conservation directly and classify the new stress as vacuum energy, scalar pressure, anisotropic stress, dust-like response, torsional contribution, or higher-derivative correction.

### 11.7 Calculation G — effective-field-theory matching

Integrate out heavy selector and internal modes. Match onto Standard Model Effective Field Theory plus gravity. Apply collider, flavour, precision electroweak, Lorentz-violation, clock, fifth-force, and equivalence-principle bounds before designing a new experiment.

### 11.8 Calculation H — one correlated observable

Choose the smallest surviving portal and calculate one coefficient that appears in both a laboratory amplitude and a gravitational or inertial response. The correlation, not merely the existence of a new parameter, is what can distinguish this programme from a generic extension.

## 12. Candidate observable classes

These are research targets, not predictions already made.

### 12.1 Algebraically restricted flavour structure

If the invariant count is sparse, the construction may imply texture zeros, mass relations, restricted mixing phases, or forbidden operators. This is a high-value frontier because it tests the division-algebraic sector close to its claimed purpose.

### 12.2 Selector–Higgs portal

A selector scalar may couple through terms such as

\[
\lambda_{VH}(V^2-v_V^2)(H^\dagger H)
\]

or an allowed low-energy equivalent. Mixing, Higgs-width shifts, fifth forces, and trace-coupled stress response could then share parameters.

### 12.3 Constrained higher-dimensional operators

The algebra may permit or forbid a distinctive subset of SMEFT operators. A useful prediction would be a relation among Wilson coefficients that survives field redefinitions and renormalisation-group running.

### 12.4 Gravitational form factors

The combined model may constrain matrix elements of (T_{\mu\nu}), nonminimal curvature couplings, torsion-sensitive spin response, or equivalence-principle deviations. Any such effect must first survive existing experimental bounds.

### 12.5 Topological boundary response

The model-local topology papers identify possible boundary signatures. The frontier task is to derive the relevant Hamiltonian from the common action and show that its boundary observable is not freely adjustable.

Galaxy rotation curves and engineered warp geometries are poor first targets: they involve too much astrophysical or geometric freedom and are too far downstream of the unproved microscopic construction.

---

## 13. Falsification conditions

The present synthesis fails, or requires material revision, if any of the following persists after the model is made precise:

1. physical negative-norm modes or an unbounded Hamiltonian;
2. failure of the reduced Cauchy problem;
3. an anomalous or incomplete chiral fermion spectrum;
4. duplicated generations or inconsistent weak couplings;
5. no allowed Standard Model Higgs/Yukawa invariant structure;
6. failure to recover the Standard Model plus gravity at low energy;
7. violation of collider, flavour, Lorentz, clock, fifth-force, or equivalence-principle bounds at the scale needed for the proposed effect;
8. separate arbitrary functions for electromagnetic visibility and gravitational response;
9. an alleged prediction that is merely a basis choice or parameter relabelling;
10. no observable distinction from the Standard Model plus generic effective field theory;
11. continued dependence on dimension matching without structural intertwiners;
12. a literal two-time branch without a complete physical quotient and first-class constraint analysis.

Null survival must be reported as a legitimate result.

---

## 14. Ordered research programme

### Phase I — canonicalisation and kinematics

1. Establish one generated claim graph as the source of truth.
2. Add supersession headers to the early symmetry monolith, Spin(2,3) compendium, and Navier–Stokes handoffs.
3. Freeze Clifford signatures, involutions, normalisations, and global-group conventions.
4. Generate the complete physical state and anomaly table.
5. Compare the proposed generation mechanisms through explicit intertwiners.

**Exit criterion:** correct, nonduplicated state content with all anomalies cancelled.

### Phase II — classical dynamics

6. Adopt the Cartan-geometric ontology as the reference model.
7. Write the minimal selector action and potential.
8. Couple the Furey-style internal module through one compatible Dirac operator.
9. Enumerate all allowed gauge, Higgs, Yukawa, and portal terms.
10. Derive the vacuum, constraints, spectrum, currents, and total stress tensor.

**Exit criterion:** a complete action with a positive, hyperbolic physical spectrum and the correct classical low-energy limit.

### Phase III — quantum consistency

11. Gauge-fix and construct the BRST complex.
12. Verify local and global anomalies.
13. Determine counterterms and power counting as an effective theory.
14. Match heavy modes onto SMEFT plus gravitational operators.
15. Run the coefficients to experimental scales.

**Exit criterion:** a controlled quantum effective theory with no unresolved symmetry obstruction.

### Phase IV — phenomenology

16. Compile existing bounds before fitting any new parameter.
17. Select the smallest portal that survives.
18. Predeclare a branch matrix including the null.
19. Calculate one correlated laboratory/gravitational observable.
20. Only then design a dedicated experiment.

**Exit criterion:** a basis-invariant prediction with uncertainty, existing constraints, and an explicit failure threshold.

### Phase V — optional cosmology

Cosmology, missing mass, vacuum structure, and global geometry enter only after the microscopic and EFT gates pass. Their parameters must be inherited from the earlier phases rather than freely refitted.

## 17. Worldview and interpretive context

The originating thesis includes a theological and philosophical arc concerning love, boundaries, responsibility, resurrection, temple, records, and admissibility. That material explains why selection, relation, witness, and response recur as organising ideas.

It should remain explicitly separated from physical warrant:

- theology may motivate which questions are asked;
- philosophy may clarify ontology and epistemology;
- mathematics determines formal consequences;
- experiment determines empirical adequacy.

No theological meaning establishes a field equation, and no physics calculation adjudicates the theological claims. Keeping the layers distinct preserves rather than diminishes both.

The most productive philosophical contribution is the emphasis on **admissibility**: a structure becomes part of physical theory only after it is compatible with dynamics, constraints, records, and observations. This maps naturally onto the programme's modern gates without being used as scientific evidence itself.

---

The programme can now be expressed as a finite set of sharp questions.

### Geometry

1. What is the minimal (Spin(2,3)) selector action whose physical quotient is positive and hyperbolic?
2. Is the selector a gauge compensator, a dynamical order parameter, or both?
3. Does its low-energy residual produce any operator not already generic in gravitational EFT?

### Internal algebra

4. Does one exact division-algebraic module reproduce every Standard Model state, chirality, and global identification without duplication?
5. Are the three generation mechanisms equivalent, overlapping, or distinct?
6. Does the algebra reduce the Yukawa parameter space or only repackage it?

### Coupling

7. Can one Dirac operator preserve both the geometric and internal selections?
8. What portal terms are forced, allowed, or forbidden by the common stabiliser?
9. Does metric variation of the complete action yield a healthy selector stress tensor?

### Quantum theory

10. Does the BRST cohomology contain exactly the intended physical states?
11. Are all perturbative and global anomalies cancelled?
12. Is the construction technically natural under radiative corrections?

### Observation

13. What is the first coefficient predicted more narrowly than in generic EFT?
14. Which existing measurement already constrains it most strongly?
15. What experiment distinguishes the live branches from the null?

### Discrete and causal completion

16. Can a (3+1)-dimensional unitary null-streaming walk reproduce the selected chiral matter module rather than merely a free Dirac cone?
17. What becomes of the unavoidable zero- and \(\pi\)-quasienergy sectors after gauge coupling, and do they restore mirror matter?
18. Can the finite-region boundary response of such a walk close as a positive \(H_2(\mathbb R)\) carrier with \(Sp(4,\mathbb R)\) sewing?

These questions define the actual frontier. Everything else is context, scaffolding, or downstream speculation.

12. no conclusion exceeds its model-local authority.
13. any proposed discrete completion explicitly counts both zero- and \(\pi\)-quasienergy sectors and verifies gauge covariance, index and anomaly flow.

Until then, the honest programme label is:

> **A mathematically rich, branch-aware research laboratory for constrained Spin(2,3)-related geometry and division-algebraic internal structure, with several exact local results but no admitted fundamental or empirical completion.**

The remaining join is dynamical: derive a renormalized neutral-plus-charged spectral kernel and determine whether face/flavor factorization survives running and thresholds.

The connection version of that join now has a decisive null test. The flavor graph carries \(\mathcal A=\frac12VdV^\dagger\) and generically nonzero curvature on a parameter space with two noncommuting directions. A single energy or RG scale has no curvature two-form, so it cannot by itself support the proposed local “twist.” Exact face--flavor tensor factorization also forces every mixed curvature component to vanish. Nonzero mixed curvature is therefore a clean target: it would certify nonfactorizable running or a true face/flavor intertwiner rather than parallel bookkeeping.

The finite-pole limitation is no longer a formal impasse. Positive matrix spectral quadrature produces a convergent sequence of rational Herglotz kernels and finite Hermitian dilations for a continuum response while preserving positive residues at every order. The worked noncommuting density converges off its cut and makes the interpretation boundary explicit: growing pole counts measure approximation rank, while only refinement-stable isolated analytic structures can be considered bound-state or resonance candidates. The remaining Standard-Model input is an actual gauge-invariant/OPE spectral density for the chosen source pair.

The nonconserved incidence source now has an exact classical completion rather than an unspecified renormalization warning. Its Yukawa violation is the quotient class \((10,6,2,1)\in\mathbb R^7/\ker\mathcal V\), and four Yukawa spurions with compensating charges restore an extended Ward identity. This produces a minimal tree-level operator set containing the two vector insertions and four Yukawa-breaking operators. It does not by itself generate curvature: the one-generation spurion phases commute, so non-Abelian geometry requires genuine matrix-valued flavor.

The Ward-compatible connection has also been completely typed. Its four-dimensional algebra is a dilation acting on a Heisenberg nilradical, and its central curvature is generated by the wedge of source shear and mixed-contact transport. But fixed positive UV normalization removes the dilation and shear, leaving only two commuting contact translations; fixed contacts remove those as well. This closes the non-Abelian within-theory source-twist branch under continuous \(B-L\). Any viable twist must instead live in flavor, theory-space moduli, calibrated boundary contacts or a broken-\(B-L\) completion.

Majorana breaking does not automatically restore the full group. If the old two-source kernel is provisionally retained and one UV slope fixed, the remnant is \(\mathfrak{so}(2)\ltimes Sym_2(\mathbb R)_{\rm contact}\cong\mathbb R\oplus\mathfrak e(2)\). Fixed contacts leave one compact rotation, while two generic UV slopes eliminate it. The actual infrared carrier remains pinned because the order parameter, longitudinal mode and Majorana operator must be included before source closure can be claimed.

Three-generation flavor now supplies the first exact nontrivial curvature invariant. For the spurion graph generated by \(H_u\) and \(H_d\), \(\mathcal F_{ud}=\frac14[H_u,H_d]\), and its determinant/cubic trace is precisely the Jarlskog obstruction up to normalization. It reverses under CP and vanishes under the familiar phase or degeneracy limits. The loop/twist language therefore has a mathematically and physically recognized realization in complex flavor space, though not yet in the \(Spin(2,3)\) face connection.

The lepton sector forces a further distinction. Hermitian PMNS curvature sees Dirac CP but cancels Majorana phases exactly. A full Takagi mass instead admits a canonical positive rank-three Nambu residue whose anomalous block retains those phases. Allowing all Hermitian observables on its support produces \(H_3(\mathbb C)\) with conformal algebra \(\mathfrak{su}(3,3)\). Thus Majorana closure can enlarge the carrier beyond the desired rank-two real system.

This enlargement reveals a precise nesting rather than an unrelated competitor. With \(e=\operatorname{diag}(1,1,0)\),
\[
H_2(\mathbb R)=\left(eH_3(\mathbb C)e\right)^{\rm conjugation},
\]
so \(H_2(\mathbb R)\subset H_2(\mathbb C)\subset H_3(\mathbb C)\) and \(Spin_0(2,3)\subset Spin_0(2,4)\subset SU(3,3)\) at the carrier/conformal level. After a chosen \(\mathbb C\subset\mathbb O\), the ladder continues into \(H_3(\mathbb O)\). The central selection problem is now sharper: explain why physical observables remain on the real rank-two Peirce face rather than exploring the full rank-three complex corner.

A canonical positive selector now exists: \(\mathcal E(X)=\operatorname{Re}(eXe)\) is an idempotent Jordan/triple-module retraction onto the \(Spin(2,3)\) face. It is necessarily lossy, annihilating positive third-face states. A large third-face gap gives a controlled dynamical approximation to the Peirce compression and removes five transverse directions with inverse-gap error, but it lands only on \(H_2(\mathbb C)\). The remaining imaginary direction demands a genuinely different real-structure mechanism.

That second mechanism has a sharp quantum boundary. Direct real projection is positive on the Jordan cone but not completely positive on a local complex quantum system; every nonzero transpose admixture has a negative antisymmetric Choi witness. Conjugate-topology doubling resolves this: encode \(A\) with \(\bar A\), implement conjugation as a unitary exchange and twirl. The resulting selector is completely positive, trace preserving and unital on the doubled system. Exact \(H_2(\mathbb R)\) selection still requires topology superselection or a gap for off-diagonal topology coherences, making the absence of mirror propagation the decisive action-level test.

The present high-value frontier is therefore no longer “find a formal Furey/\(Spin(2,3)\) resemblance.” It is:

1. derive a positive two-face kernel from an action, Green function or spectral measure;
2. prove the second topology face is auxiliary, boundary or redundant rather than mirror matter;
3. promote the exact quadratic commutation of gauge reduction and \(H_2(\mathbb R)\) compression to interacting spectral kernels;
4. derive the scale-dependent state on \(\mathbb C^5\) from stress–energy, a spectral sum rule or an operational source;
5. calculate the first observable relation narrower than generic effective field theory.

The stop-everything condition is equally direct: if no positive covariant kernel naturally carries the two topology faces without adding propagating mirrors, the exact algebraic bridge does not become a physical theory.

Primary records for this update are [documents 613–689](../0600-0699/613_conjugate_twisted_furey_ternary_incidence_uniquely_selects_the_lepton_doublet.md) and the corresponding executable certificates in `scripts/`.

The combined rank/exchange selector is now explicit and completely positive on the doubled carrier. Prakash and Ramya's newly sourced formulation of a Standard-Model generation as an integral triorthogonal code sharpens its possible physical origin: their construction has two Dirac-paired seven-state faces, a weak-isospin linking row, a neutrino seed and a punctured logical partner. This is not yet the same as conjugate-topology doubling. The decisive new gate is an explicit intertwiner carrying the Dirac-pair involution and linker into the selector's exchange and topology-parity actions. See [Prakash–Ramya at the current frontier](../0600-0699/689_prakash_ramya_at_the_current_frontier_two_doublings_one_cubic_residue_and_the_missing_intertwiner.md).

The support and observable parts of that gate are now exact. The Dirac involution is \(\Theta(X)=PX^TP\) on oriented Furey arrows, while the topology real structure is \(\rho(X)=P\bar XP\). They differ on arrows but coincide on Hermitian observables. The transpose-twist is not completely positive locally; its paired two-block exchange dilation is CP, trace preserving and projects onto the \(\Theta\)-fixed observable. The remaining problem is to derive the paired block relation and preserve lost TRO orientation in interaction data. See [Dirac face exchange and the CP topology real structure](../0600-0699/690_dirac_face_exchange_becomes_the_cp_topology_real_structure_only_on_hermitian_observables.md).

An exact action-level realization of the topology twirl is now also available. The Lindbladian \(\mathcal L=\gamma_x(\operatorname{Ad}_S-I)+\gamma_z(\operatorname{Ad}_Z-I)\) converges exponentially to the Pauli twirl, with sector decay rates \(0,-2\gamma_z,-2\gamma_x,-2(\gamma_x+\gamma_z)\). Its fixed algebra is \(I_{\rm topology}\otimes M_{\rm carrier}\): it depolarizes the topology qubit rather than selecting a pure symmetric topology state. This is a controlled open-system or gauge-reduction mechanism, not a Hamiltonian mirror-mass gap. See [the topology Pauli Lindbladian](../0600-0699/691_the_topology_pauli_lindbladian_converges_exactly_to_the_cp_selector_but_depolarizes_the_topology_qubit.md).

The two dynamical stages now have a common quantitative regime. For a conjugate-paired projected resolvent, the total Frobenius error is bounded by the sum of a pairing defect, a Neumann-controlled inverse-gap term and an exponentially decaying topology term. The controls are independent: \(\Delta\to\infty\) does not repair pairing, and \(t\to\infty\) does not repair a nearby heavy pole. The factorized finite model has no Trotter error because carrier and topology superoperators commute; interacting gauge/Yukawa terms may add a commutator correction. See [the joint selector error bound](../0600-0699/692_the_full_two_stage_selector_has_a_joint_inverse_gap_and_exponential_time_error_bound.md).

The previously abstract topology Pauli algebra is now internal to the complete Standard-Model generation. Dirac exchange supplies \(S\), while \(Z_{\rm chir}=(2T_{3R})^2-(2T_{3L})^2=q_4q_5\) supplies parity; they square to one and anticommute. Hence the sixteen states factor exactly as a topology doublet times eight Dirac pairs. Puncturing \(\nu^c\) removes one \(Z=+1\) partner while retaining \(\nu\), producing the code's \(7+8\) split and destroying closure under \(S\). The logical coordinate is therefore exactly the completion required by the topology algebra. The next gate is the total Higgs/Yukawa grading, not discovery of a parity operator. See [weak-isospin topology parity](../0600-0699/693_weak_isospin_squared_supplies_the_exact_topology_parity_and_nuc_puncturing_breaks_the_pauli_pair.md).

That interaction grading is now closed. \(Z_{\rm chir}=q_4q_5\) is exactly the \(SU(2)_L\) center character, so the Higgs doublet is forced to be odd and all four one-generation Yukawa monomials are even; the Majorana term, Weinberg operator and Higgs potential are even as well. This removes \(Z\) as an independent noise mechanism on gauge-invariant observables. Dirac exchange \(S\), however, swaps left and right Cartan data and is not gauge-covariant on bare Standard-Model fermions. The frontier has narrowed to a Higgs/gauge-dressed exchange or an enlarged left-right/\(SO(10)\) origin. See [topology parity as the weak center](../0600-0699/694_topology_parity_is_the_su2l_center_all_yukawas_are_even_but_dirac_exchange_requires_enlarged_covariance.md).

A Higgs- and Yukawa-polar-dressed exchange now closes that compatibility gate away from defects. The normalized weak frame \((\widetilde h,h)\), combined with the polar unitaries \(U_u,U_d\), defines a unitary face map \(D_h\) and involution \(S_h\) satisfying \(S_h^2=1\), \(\{S_h,Z_{\rm chir}\}=0\) and \([S_h,H_M]=0\). An explicit three-family example retains a nonzero CKM-type commutator determinant. The boundary is important: because \(S_h\) is built from the Yukawas, this proves compatibility rather than prediction, and it is noncanonical at Higgs zeros or Yukawa kernels. See [the Higgs-polar dressed exchange](../0600-0699/695_a_higgs_and_yukawa_polar_dressed_dirac_exchange_closes_the_pauli_algebra_without_erasing_ckm_cp.md).

The Higgs-zero defect now has an exact topology. A normalized nonzero Higgs is an \(SU(2)\cong S^3\) frame, while its projective direction is the Hopf map to \(S^2\). The degree-one frame on an \(S^3\) link has Hopf invariant one and cannot extend through an isolated four-real-dimensional zero without vanishing. This locates the Hopf structure in the orientation bundle of the dressed exchange. It does not produce a protected Standard-Model string or monopole because \(\pi_1(S^3)=\pi_2(S^3)=0\). See [the Higgs-frame Hopf defect](../0600-0699/696_the_higgs_dressed_exchange_has_a_hopf_frame_defect_but_not_a_topologically_protected_standard_model_string.md).

The other dressed-exchange defect, a rank-two neutrino Yukawa matrix, is now classified. Its polar partial isometry admits a \(U(1)\) family of unitary extensions; every extension closes the topology Pauli algebra and commutes with the rank-two mass operator, and all agree on the massive support. A nonzero complex mass for the kernel pair fixes the extension phase. This separates removal of \(\nu^c\), which destroys the pair, from a zero Dirac singular value, which retains the pair but leaves its phase free. See [the neutrino-kernel exchange ambiguity](../0600-0699/697_a_massless_dirac_neutrino_leaves_a_u1_exchange_ambiguity_while_puncturing_nuc_removes_the_pair.md).

Majorana completion creates a sharper symmetry fork. A zero Takagi column has \(U(1)\) freedom, while a nonzero complex Majorana mass fixes half its phase modulo sign. Yet for \(\mathcal M=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}m_L&m_D\\m_D&m_R\end{smallmatrix}\right)\), exact face exchange holds iff \(m_L=m_R\). The ordinary type-I choice \(m_L=0,m_R\ne0\) lifts the Dirac degeneracy but breaks topology exchange. It must therefore be treated as an exchange-breaking spurion/order parameter, an ultraviolet left-right breaking, or a departure from exact selector symmetry. See [the Majorana Takagi/exchange fork](../0600-0699/698_a_majorana_mass_reduces_takagi_phase_freedom_but_a_type_i_seesaw_breaks_exact_topology_exchange.md).

The seesaw cannot replace the topology selector dynamically. Its light eigenstate has \(\langle Z_{\rm chir}\rangle=-M_R/\sqrt{M_R^2+4m_D^2}\to-1\) and remains a pure topology state, while the Pauli twirl has vanishing topology Bloch vector. The two mechanisms may share a Schur-complement formula, but they act on different factors: seesaw decoupling selects one matter face, whereas the CP selector removes the topology label from observables. See [type-I seesaw decoupling is not the topology twirl](../0600-0699/699_type_i_seesaw_decoupling_selects_one_face_and_cannot_be_the_topology_twirl.md).

The exchange-breaking Majorana component now has an explicit order-parameter model. An exchange-invariant double well in \(m_-=(m_R-m_L)/2\) selects the two one-sided seesaw orientations and, for global exchange, supports an exact \(\phi^4\) kink with tension \(4\sqrt{2b}v^3/3\). If exchange is a Weyl gauge redundancy, those minima are one gauge orbit and the global wall is unphysical; defects must be recomputed from the full vacuum quotient. A fixed spurion gives no wall and no explanation of the sign. See [the exchange-odd Majorana order parameter](../0700-0799/700_the_exchange_odd_majorana_order_parameter_has_an_exact_global_kink_but_no_wall_if_exchange_is_weyl_gauge.md).

The long-pinned cubic-residue branch now has an exact central theorem. For \(X=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&w\\w^\dagger&c\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in H_3(\mathbb C)\), \(\det X=c\det A-w^\dagger\operatorname{adj}(A)w\), and the directional derivative of the cubic along the discarded idempotent is exactly \(\det A\). Hence the rank-two Lorentz norm is the polarized residue of the rank-three cubic. A heavy face gives an inverse-gap correction, while the real selector changes the determinant by the square of the removed imaginary coordinate. This yields the exact chain from cubic Jordan norm to the \((1,2)\) quadratic whose conformal group is \(Spin_0(2,3)\). See [the polarized cubic residue theorem](../0700-0799/701_polarizing_the_rank_three_jordan_cubic_along_the_discarded_face_gives_the_spin23_rank_two_determinant.md).

The theorem has now been lifted to \(H_3(\mathbb O)\) with explicit octonion multiplication. The real part of the associator vanishes, making the exceptional cubic bracket-safe, and polarization yields \(H_2(\mathbb O)\) exactly. This initially has signature \((1,9)\), not the desired \((1,2)\). The latter is the entrywise \(G_2\)-fixed real slice, whereas Furey's color stabilizer fixes a complex subalgebra. The new sharp frontier is therefore a factorized action in which internal matter retains the \(SU(3)\)-selected complex direction while external observables are reduced to the \(G_2\)-fixed real carrier. See [the exceptional polarization theorem](../0700-0799/702_the_exceptional_jordan_cubic_polarizes_to_h2o_and_reaches_spin23_only_on_the_g2_fixed_real_slice.md).

## 20. Final assessment

The corpus has progressed from expansive theoretical storytelling into a credible research-management framework. Its most important achievement is not the proof of a final theory; it is the construction of a disciplined environment in which attractive ideas can fail without being silently discarded or rhetorically promoted.

The central synthesis remains plausible enough to investigate and constrained enough to falsify:

- Furey's programme offers a serious answer to why Standard Model-like internal representations may arise from a compact algebraic source.
- \(Spin(2,3)\) offers a conservative Cartan-geometric route for asking why the observed Lorentzian sector is selected.
- Stress–energy and the common action force these proposals to inhabit the same dynamical world.

The research frontier is now sharply located at their intersection. If the physical module fails, the internal proposal fails. If the selector spectrum contains ghosts, the geometric proposal fails. If the action passes but produces only arbitrary generic-EFT coefficients, the unification claim loses predictive force. If a sparse invariant structure survives all gates and fixes a correlated observable, the programme advances from mathematical architecture to physical theory.

That is the correct standard: not whether the structures are beautiful or recurrent, but whether one explicit model survives representation theory, dynamics, quantum consistency, and experiment at the same time.

## Frontier update: a finite \(G_2\) Reynolds selector has a CP dilation

The exceptional-to-real descent is no longer only a fixed-point observation. Give the seven imaginary octonion units their nonzero \(\mathbb F_2^3\) Fano degrees. The eight characters of that grading act by sign automorphisms and form a subgroup \((\mathbb Z_2)^3\subset G_2\). Their Reynolds average is exactly

\[
\frac18\sum_{g\in(\mathbb Z_2)^3}g(z)=\operatorname{Re}z.
\]

Entrywise on \(H_2(\mathbb O)\), this is the positive, unital, trace-preserving idempotent

\[
\begin{pmatrix}a&z\\\bar z&b\end{pmatrix}
\longmapsto
\begin{pmatrix}a&\operatorname{Re}z\\\operatorname{Re}z&b\end{pmatrix}.
\]

In the left-regular \(16\times16\) real block representation, the same average is a random-orthogonal-unitary channel with Kraus operators \(U_g/\sqrt8\), and it sends the encoded exceptional rank-two carrier to eight identical copies of \(H_2(\mathbb R)\).

This closes the existence problem for a CP dilation of the selector, but not its uniqueness or dynamics. On the full matrix algebra the channel fixes more than the encoded \(H_2(\mathbb R)\) sector. The next decisive calculation is the exact fixed-algebra decomposition and the additional condition required to force all surviving character blocks to agree. See [the finite \(G_2\)-subgroup CP selector](../0700-0799/703_an_eight_element_octonion_automorphism_twirl_is_a_cp_dilation_of_the_h2o_to_h2r_selector.md).

That decomposition is now exact. On the doubled left-regular space, the finite twirl fixes \(\bigoplus_8M_2(\mathbb R)\): its symmetric range has dimension \(24\), while synchronized \(H_2(\mathbb R)\) has dimension three. Full Haar \(G_2\) reduces the symmetric commutant to dimension six but cannot impose equality between its scalar and imaginary \(M_2\) responses, because every octonion automorphism fixes \(1\). Thus “average over more \(G_2\)” is now closed as a route to an exact ambient range. The branch must derive the Jordan encoding as a physical superselection sector or supply a non-automorphism synchronization mechanism. See [the fixed-algebra and synchronization no-go](../0700-0799/704_the_finite_g2_selector_fixed_algebra_is_eight_m2_blocks_and_symmetry_alone_cannot_synchronize_them.md).

The parent exceptional cubic does not automatically provide that missing mechanism. On the six-dimensional Haar-\(G_2\) symmetric commutant, infinitely many invariant extensions agree with the Jordan determinant on the synchronized slice but have arbitrary transverse curvature \(\kappa\). With the representation-weighted metric, the three difference modes have \(m^2=8\kappa/7\), so the same on-slice determinant permits a stable gap, a flat sector or an instability. This closes the shortcut from “cubic residue equals determinant” to “cubic dynamics selects the determinant carrier.” A canonical off-slice action or fundamental-Jordan postulate is still required. See [the transverse synchronization underdetermination theorem](../0700-0799/705_the_exceptional_cubic_does_not_by_itself_fix_the_transverse_synchronization_gap.md).

A Clifford-algebra reformulation now supplies such an exact kinematic extension. The left-regular \(16\)-dimensional realization of \(H_2(\mathbb O)\cong V_9\) carries nine symmetric anticommuting Clifford generators. The face-difference and real-link generators are \(Z\otimes I_8\) and \(X\otimes I_8\). Averaging conjugation by the \(64\) real three-rebit Pauli operators on the multiplicity factor implements \(\operatorname{id}_{M_2}\otimes\operatorname{Depol}_8\). Its full range is exactly \(M_2(\mathbb R)\otimes I_8\), and its symmetric range is exactly \(H_2(\mathbb R)\). This evades rather than contradicts the \(G_2\) no-go: Pauli multiplicity conjugations are Clifford-dilation symmetries, not octonion automorphisms. See [the exact Clifford-multiplicity selector](../0700-0799/706_a_clifford_multiplicity_conditional_expectation_exactly_selects_h2r_without_the_g2_fixed_range_excess.md).

The selector now has an exact six-jump Lindblad realization. Three rebit \(X_i,Z_i\) conjugations give Pauli-word eigenvalues \(-2\gamma(|a|+|b|)\), a Liouvillian gap \(2\gamma\), and Hilbert–Schmidt error bounded by \(e^{-2\gamma t}\). The symmetric steady algebra is exactly the three-dimensional carrier. This also exposes a cross-branch typing constraint: the earlier topology Pauli Lindbladian erases the full algebra of its two-level factor. If that topology qubit is identified with the Clifford face, composing the two selectors collapses \(H_2(\mathbb R)\) to scalars. They are compatible only when topology is a separate external duplicate. See [the Clifford Lindbladian and face-typing obstruction](../0700-0799/707_a_six_jump_clifford_lindbladian_converges_to_h2r_with_exact_gap_but_must_not_depolarize_the_same_face.md).

The corpus-wide typing audit sharpens this into four distinct factors: external topology duplicate \(T\), Clifford face \(F\), Clifford multiplicity \(C\), and eightfold matter Dirac-pair label \(M\). Separate \(T\) and \(F\) give joint symmetric range \(H_2(\mathbb R)\); identifying them gives only scalars. Likewise, \(C\) and \(M\) both have dimension eight but cannot be identified under the full Pauli noise action without erasing Standard-Model observables. The missing synthesis arrow is now a gauge-compatible response map from matter observables to the Clifford face, not a dimension-matching state-space equality. See [the topology/Clifford factor audit](../0700-0799/708_the_topology_face_and_clifford_carrier_must_be_separately_typed_or_the_joint_selector_collapses_to_scalars.md).

The normalizer/Furey comparison now resolves the response-line degeneracy. After fixing the diagonal Peirce direction, \(Cl_2\) choices form \(\mathbb RP^7\). Furey’s complex unit \(u\) reduces \(G_2\) to \(SU(3)_u\), but the exact common fixed space on \(\mathbb O\) is \(\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb R}\{1,u\}\), leaving a circle of invariant link directions \(q(\theta)=\cos\theta\,1+\sin\theta\,u\). Complex conjugation reduces this to the even scalar axis and odd imaginary axis; it does not select between them. See [the Clifford normalizer and Furey stabilizer calculation](../0700-0799/709_fureys_su3_stabilizer_leaves_a_circle_of_clifford_response_lines_until_conjugation_parity_selects_an_axis.md).

The odd axis cannot be rejected kinematically. A spin rotation conjugates the scalar-axis channel into a \(64\)-Kraus CP expectation retaining \(\langle u,z\rangle\). Its symmetric range is again \(H_2(\mathbb R)\), with the same determinant, cone and Lindblad spectrum. Therefore positivity, complete positivity and \(Spin(2,3)\) geometry do not choose response parity. The next genuine synthesis datum is whether the gauge-invariant Hermitian matter-to-stress kernel is even, odd or mixed under Furey conjugation. See [the exact odd-axis selector](../0700-0799/710_the_furey_odd_imaginary_axis_also_has_an_exact_cp_h2r_selector_so_positivity_does_not_choose_the_axis.md).

A conditional stress selection theorem now narrows that question. In a Lorentz-invariant vacuum, a symmetric stress tensor and a genuine spacetime pseudoscalar have no nonzero two-point tensor structure: \(\epsilon_{\mu\alpha\nu\beta}q^\alpha q^\beta=0\), and no other pseudotensor can be built from one momentum. Hence the odd axis is absent from linear vacuum stress response if Furey-odd maps to spacetime pseudoscalar. That parity intertwiner is still missing, so the result pins rather than deletes the odd branch. See [the stress–pseudoscalar two-point no-go](../0700-0799/711_symmetric_stress_cannot_linearly_source_a_spacetime_pseudoscalar_in_a_lorentz_invariant_vacuum_but_furey_odd_is_not_yet_spacetime_odd.md).

For a generic mixed response line, the exact projective CP-odd invariant is \(\mathcal I_{\rm CP}=2r_er_o/(r_e^2+r_o^2)=\sin2\theta\). A quadratic kernel \(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha&c\\c&\beta\end{smallmatrix}\right)\) gives \(\tan2\theta=2c/(\alpha-\beta)\); conjugation symmetry forbids \(c\) unless it is an odd spurion or order parameter. CKM CP therefore becomes a concrete matching question for \(c\), not a verbal explanation of the angle. See [the mixed-line projective invariant](../0700-0799/712_a_mixed_clifford_response_line_has_a_projective_cp_odd_invariant_and_requires_an_odd_cross_kernel.md).

The existing Higgs/Yukawa-dressed exchange does not supply the missing parity map. It is a complex Hermitian linear involution \(S_h\), but the naive antiunitary \(S_hK\) has square \(S_h\bar S_h\ne I\) for generic complex polar factors. A conjugate-background doubling repairs this exactly, yet the repaired matter real structure still admits both identity and \(\kappa_u\) extensions on the response plane. Thus the Higgs/Yukawa construction preserves CKM CP precisely by not fixing the Clifford parity. See [the dressed-exchange antiunitary obstruction](../0700-0799/713_the_higgs_yukawa_dressed_exchange_is_linear_not_an_antiunitary_and_does_not_select_clifford_response_parity.md).

The Prakash–Ramya paper’s matter doubling and the octonion response multiplicity now have an exact representation comparison. The full generation is \(4\cdot\mathbf1\oplus2\cdot\mathbf3\oplus2\cdot\bar{\mathbf3}\cong2\mathbb O_{\mathbb C}\) under color. But the physical conjugate-right face is \(2\cdot\mathbf1\oplus2\cdot\bar{\mathbf3}\), while the left face is \(2\cdot\mathbf1\oplus2\cdot\mathbf3\); their generic color characters differ, and no invertible complex-linear color intertwiner exists. Thus the two-octonion grouping necessarily crosses the Dirac faces. See [the color-module crossing theorem](../0700-0799/714_the_standard_model_color_module_is_two_complexified_octonion_copies_but_not_in_the_prakash_ramya_dirac_face_factorization.md).

Electroweak breaking selects the crossing. No octonion-shaped eight-dimensional submodule exists as a sum of unbroken Standard-Model irreducibles. After breaking, color plus electromagnetism leave four charge-conjugation-closed candidates; Higgs-channel homogeneity uniquely selects \(\mathcal O_{\rm up}=\{\nu,\nu^c,u,u^c\}\) and \(\mathcal O_{\rm down}=\{e,e^c,d,d^c\}\). This is an exact representation-level Furey/Prakash–Ramya bridge: the Higgs frame chooses an octonionic polarization of the generation. A universal \(Spin(2,3)\) response still requires the even/odd response vectors of the two modules to align. See [the Higgs-selected crossed octonion modules](../0700-0799/715_electroweak_breaking_and_the_higgs_frame_select_two_crossed_octonion_shaped_generation_modules.md).

That alignment is exact for channel-local Hermitian Yukawa kernels. Each \(H_f=Y_fY_f^\dagger\) has only real trace invariants, so both crossed modules source \((E,0)\) on the even Clifford axis and \(\Delta_{\rm align}=0\). One and two generations cannot supply the cubic commutator CP invariant. Three generations can generate \(\operatorname{ImTr}[H_u,H_d]^3\), but separate matching form factors generically misalign the two response lines. Thus the one-generation code naturally supports a universal even carrier at leading order, while realistic flavor CP turns universality into an additional calculable constraint. See [the channel-local alignment theorem](../0700-0799/716_channel_local_hermitian_yukawa_kernels_align_on_the_even_clifford_axis_while_cp_odd_misalignment_requires_cross_channel_flavor_data.md).

Majorana data create an earlier conditional obstruction. In the charged-lepton phase frame, \(\mathcal I^M_{ij}=\operatorname{Im}[m_{ii}m_{jj}(m_{ij}^*)^2]\) is rephasing invariant and can be nonzero with two families. It belongs to the up/neutral crossed module. If it has a nonzero odd-axis matching coefficient while the down/charged response stays even, then \(\Delta_{\rm align}=-E_{\rm down}F_M\mathcal I^M\ne0\). The invariant alone does not prove such matching, but any explicit nonzero coefficient would falsify a single universal response line unless a cross-channel alignment mechanism exists. See [the Majorana alignment obstruction](../0700-0799/717_a_majorana_cp_invariant_exists_with_two_families_and_generically_misaligns_the_up_neutral_response_if_it_matches_the_odd_axis.md).

That pair invariant is only invariant under residual rephasings after the charged-lepton frame has been chosen; it is not by itself a full weak-basis scalar. The correct two-family-capable completion can be written
\[
I_2=\operatorname{ImTr}(H_\ell H_\nu G_{\ell\nu}),\qquad
G_{\ell\nu}=m_\nu H_\ell^*m_\nu^\dagger .
\]
It is invariant under arbitrary unitary weak-basis transformations, can be nonzero for a complex symmetric two-family Majorana mass, and vanishes in the real control. It has post-EWSB mass dimension eight and is quartic in both the Majorana and charged-lepton mass/Yukawa insertions. The obstruction is therefore genuinely cross-sector and basis independent, but parametrically much higher order than the earlier fixed-frame expression suggested. See [the weak-basis completion and spurion-order audit](../0700-0799/718_the_majorana_alignment_obstruction_requires_a_charged_lepton_weak_basis_invariant_and_is_high_spurion_order.md).

The same invariant also clarifies the vacuum stress boundary. The earlier stress–pseudoscalar theorem assumed no odd background. If an explicit parity/CP intertwiner sends the Furey-odd coordinate to a compatible CP-odd Lorentz scalar, then \(I_2\) supplies an odd spurion and the conserved kernel \(I_2F_u(q^2)(q_\mu q_\nu-q^2\eta_{\mu\nu})\) is symmetry-allowed. This is a conditional reopening, not a nonzero matching result: neither the intertwiner nor \(F_u\) has been derived. See [the CP-spurion reopening audit](../0700-0799/719_a_cp_odd_flavor_spurion_conditionally_reopens_the_vacuum_stress_odd_response_but_does_not_supply_the_furey_parity_intertwiner.md).

The local-source search now has a dimension ladder. A broken-phase Majorana odd bilinear is dimension three but is neither unbroken-gauge-invariant nor weak-basis invariant by itself. Gauge topological densities and odd Yukawa combinations occur at dimension four, but the former have no ordinary constant-coefficient bulk metric variation and the latter can carry removable phases. The direct unbroken Majorana target is the dimension-five odd Weinberg combination \(i(\mathcal O_5-\mathcal O_5^\dagger)\). Its low operator dimension coexists with the high order of the physical coefficient \(I_2\). This makes Weinberg/Furey covariance—not raw dimensional availability—the next selection test. See [the CP-odd operator ladder](../0700-0799/720_the_lowest_dimension_cp_odd_response_sources_form_a_gauge_phase_and_metric_relevance_ladder.md).

That covariance test has a precise partial solution. With the Majorana/Nambu block \(\mathcal M=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&m_\nu\\m_\nu^\dagger&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\) and \(\widehat H_\ell=\operatorname{diag}(H_\ell,H_\ell^*)\), the upper compression of the ordinary TRO triple \(\{\mathcal M,\widehat H_\ell,\mathcal M\}=\mathcal M\widehat H_\ell^\dagger\mathcal M\) is exactly \(G_{\ell\nu}=m_\nu H_\ell^*m_\nu^\dagger\). The identity is weak-basis covariant. It lives in the Nambu linking TRO, not in the bare one-generation Furey matter TRO; the two may be tensor-amplified, but contracting them to the Clifford odd axis remains open. See [the Nambu TRO compression theorem](../0700-0799/721_the_majorana_weak_basis_word_is_one_nambu_tro_compression_but_not_an_internal_product_of_the_bare_furey_matter_tro.md).

The remaining linear contraction now has an exact representation-theoretic fork. For one CP-odd scalar source and response parity \(\operatorname{diag}(+1,-1)\), the CP-equivariant Hom space is one-dimensional and its image is exactly the odd axis \(\Gamma_u\). Symmetry selects the direction but not the coefficient. If the full response \(Spin(2)\) rotation is imposed as an unbroken gauge symmetry while the source is a singlet, that Hom space collapses to zero. A physical response line therefore requires the rotation to be a kinematic normalizer, a broken/global symmetry, or to act nontrivially on an enlarged source multiplet. See [the response-intertwiner and \(Spin(2)\) obstruction](../0700-0799/722_cp_equivariance_uniquely_sends_one_odd_flavor_scalar_to_the_odd_response_axis_but_unbroken_response_spin2_forces_the_map_to_zero.md).

There are, however, two distinct rotations that must not share that name. The fixed-carrier automorphism rotates the traceless coordinates \((d,q)\) inside one selected \(H_2(\mathbb R)\). The embedding-selector rotation fixes \(d\) and rotates \((1,u)\), moving among different embedded carriers. Their generators do not commute and generate \(\mathfrak{so}(3)\) on \(\operatorname{span}\{d,1,u\}\). Furey conjugation commutes with the even fixed-carrier rotation but reflects the selector rotation. Consequently, the zero-map theorem applies only if the selector circle survives as an exact gauge redundancy; it does not follow from the ordinary automorphism group of the chosen Jordan carrier. See [the two-\(Spin(2)\) normalizer audit](../0700-0799/723_the_fixed_carrier_spin2_and_the_response_embedding_selector_spin2_are_distinct_and_furey_conjugation_breaks_only_the_latter.md).

The minimal coupled stabilizer is now explicit. For \(K(s)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha&cs\\cs&\beta\end{smallmatrix}\right)\), with \(s\) a CP-odd weak-basis scalar, the connected selector stabilizer survives only when \(\alpha=\beta\) and \(cs=0\). Otherwise the response-line eigenvalue gap is \(\Delta=\sqrt{(\alpha-\beta)^2+4c^2s^2}\). Spurionic CP relates \(K(s)\) to \(K(-s)\), but a fixed nonzero background is not CP invariant. If the selector circle was a physical global symmetry, \(m_\theta^2=\Delta\) is the angular pseudo-Goldstone mass for canonical Cartesian kinetics; if it was only an embedding degeneracy, \(\Delta\) is response stiffness. See [the minimal coupled-kernel stabilizer theorem](../0700-0799/724_a_minimal_cp_spurion_kernel_generically_removes_the_selector_circle_and_gives_the_response_angle_a_calculable_curvature.md).

The required weight-two source pair is already present in the full complex Majorana trace \(Z_2=\operatorname{Tr}(H_\ell H_\nu G_{\ell\nu})=E_2+iI_2\). The symmetric traceless matrix \(Q(Z_2)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}E_2&I_2\\I_2&-E_2\end{smallmatrix}\right)\) obeys \(CQ(Z_2)C=Q(Z_2^*)\) and \(R(\varphi)^TQ(Z_2)R(\varphi)=Q(e^{-2i\varphi}Z_2)\). If one complex-linear matching coefficient multiplies this whole tensor, then \(2\theta=\arg Z_2\) and \(\Delta=2|g_2Z_2|\). CP covariance alone permits independent even and odd coefficients, so this prediction is an explicit new gate rather than a conclusion. See [the complex Majorana weight-two pair](../0700-0799/725_the_full_complex_majorana_trace_supplies_a_canonical_cp_even_odd_pair_with_selector_weight_two.md).

The Hom-space classification makes that gate exact. General real CP-equivariant maps are \(\operatorname{diag}(g_E,g_I)\), a two-dimensional family. Requiring preservation of the source multiplication by \(i\) and the target Furey rotation \(1\mapsto u\) forces \(g_E=g_I\); anti-complex sewing instead gives \(g_E=-g_I\). Equal Clifford norms make the complex-linear ray a canonical algebraic isometry once \(i\leftrightarrow u\) is chosen, but no dynamics yet selects it. See [the CP/complex-linear intertwiner classification](../0700-0799/726_cp_equivariance_allows_two_response_coefficients_while_the_furey_complex_structure_selects_a_canonical_common_coefficient_ray.md).

The existing Hermitian exchanged-TRO response does not close that gap. It is exactly phase blind in its repeated state slot, \(R^{\rm H}_{e^{i\phi}x}(z)=R^{\rm H}_x(z)\), while remaining complex linear in the probe slot. Hence it cannot transport the phase of \(Z_2\) if that phase is encoded only in the matter state.
