It can carry \(Z_2\) only after the Nambu linking trace supplies it as separately typed complex source data; the final Hermitian realification still decides whether one or two coefficients survive. See [the exchanged-TRO slot-linearity audit](../0700-0799/727_the_hermitian_exchanged_tro_is_state_phase_blind_and_can_transport_z2_only_through_a_separately_typed_complex_probe.md).

That realification has a minimal-rank theorem. No nonzero complex-linear map from a scalar \(z\in\mathbb C\) can produce a Hermitian observable for every complex input; naïve Hermitianization keeps only \(\operatorname{Re}z\). Retaining both quadratures positively requires a carrier coordinate \(t\) and the lift \(X(t,Z_2)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}t+E_2&I_2\\I_2&t-E_2\end{smallmatrix}\right)\). It is positive exactly for \(t\ge|Z_2|\), and \(\det X=t^2-|Z_2|^2\). Thus complex relational data plus Hermitian positivity plus one minimal carrier coordinate reconstruct the \(H_2(\mathbb R)\) cone; the physical origin of \(t\) remains open. See [the positive two-quadrature realification theorem](../0700-0799/728_a_complex_probe_cannot_map_nontrivially_and_completely_positively_directly_to_hermitian_observables_but_its_minimal_two_quadrature_lift_is_h2r.md).

The carrier coordinate now has a canonical spectral candidate. For two real, gauge-neutral, spin-projected physical amplitudes \(a,b\), their positive Gram density has \(t=(\|a\|^2+\|b\|^2)/2\), \(E=(\|a\|^2-\|b\|^2)/2\), and \(I=\langle a,b\rangle\). Cauchy–Schwarz is exactly \(t^2-E^2-I^2\ge0\). Thus \(t\) is half the trace—the average total positive spectral weight—and is additive under positive spectral sums. What remains is the form-factor theorem \((E,I)=g(\operatorname{Re}Z_2,\operatorname{Im}Z_2)\) with common dimensions and conserved probes. See [the spectral Gram origin of the carrier coordinate](../0700-0799/729_half_the_trace_of_a_positive_two_probe_spectral_gram_is_the_canonical_carrier_coordinate_but_z2_matching_remains_a_form_factor_gate.md).

The form-factor ledger is now fixed. An unfactored four-dimensional stress two-point spectral kernel has mass dimension four, whereas \([Z_2]=8\), so \([g_{TT}]=-4\); after factoring an explicit \(q^4\) tensor, the reduced coefficient instead has dimension \(-8\). The invariant contains exactly four Majorana-mass and four charged-lepton-mass insertions. In the unbroken EFT it scales as \(v^{12}Y_\ell^4C_5^4\), and in a type-I seesaw the four \(C_5\) factors contain at least eight neutrino Yukawas and four inverse heavy masses. This is an analytic spurion lower bound, not a loop-order theorem. See [the stress-matching dimension and insertion audit](../0700-0799/730_z2_matching_to_a_full_stress_spectral_kernel_requires_dimension_minus_four_and_at_least_four_majorana_plus_four_charged_lepton_mass_insertions.md).

The obvious lower-degree trace shortcut is now closed. In the real-coefficient cyclic trace algebra generated by the Hermitian covariants \(H_\ell,H_\nu,G_{\ell\nu}\), every word below weighted mass dimension eight is cyclically equivalent to its reverse and therefore has real trace. The first reversal-asymmetric necklace is \(H_\ell H_\nu G_{\ell\nu}\), whose imaginary part is \(I_2\). Other standard invariants begin at dimensions ten, twelve or eighteen. This is a scoped minimality theorem, not a complete classification with additional covariants, rational projectors or external flavor labels. See [the minimal CP-odd trace-word audit](../0700-0799/731_i2_is_the_lowest_degree_cp_odd_single_trace_in_the_standard_hl_hnu_glnu_covariant_algebra_so_that_shortcut_is_closed.md).

External LNV flavor labels do open a lower-order route. For two charged flavors, exhaustive rephasing-charge balance shows the first genuinely complex invariant of the Majorana-exchange kernel is the degree-four quartet \(J^F_{\alpha\beta}=\operatorname{Im}[F_{\alpha\alpha}F_{\beta\beta}(F_{\alpha\beta}^*)^2]\); none exists at degree two or three. In the deep-Euclidean limit it becomes \(-\operatorname{Im}[m_{\alpha\alpha}m_{\beta\beta}(m_{\alpha\beta}^*)^2]/Q^8\). It needs four Majorana-exchange amplitudes but no explicit charged-lepton mass spurions because the external charged leptons supply the flavor frame. Its sign still requires coherent phase tomography, not isolated rates. See [the minimal LNV amplitude quartet](../0700-0799/732_a_two_flavor_lnv_amplitude_quartet_is_the_minimal_rephasing_invariant_majorana_cp_observable_and_avoids_charged_lepton_mass_spurions.md).

The minimal positive tomography network is also explicit. Two composite paths \(p=F_{\alpha\alpha}F_{\beta\beta}\) and \(q=F_{\alpha\beta}^2\) have the same rephasing charge and the same two-copy external flavor multiset. Their invariant coherence has \(\operatorname{Im}(pq^*)=J^F_{\alpha\beta}\). The compression \(R_{pq}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}t+\operatorname{Re}pq^*&\operatorname{Im}pq^*\\\operatorname{Im}pq^*&t-\operatorname{Re}pq^*\end{smallmatrix}\right)\), with \(t=(|p|^2+|q|^2)/2\), is positive and has determinant \((|p|^2-|q|^2)^2/4\). Two phase settings extract the odd quadrature. The physical four-source/four-lepton coherence remains open. See [the two-copy LNV (H_2(\mathbb R)\) interferometer](../0700-0799/733_a_two_copy_lnv_interferometer_embeds_the_minimal_majorana_cp_quartet_in_a_positive_h2r_carrier.md).

Fermionic Wick algebra supplies that coherence conditionally. A coherent four-mode Majorana pair source has Pfaffian amplitude \(K_{12}K_{34}-K_{13}K_{24}+K_{14}K_{23}=Pp+Qq\) for final flavors \((\alpha,\alpha,\beta,\beta)\). Its rate contains the required \(pq^*\) cross term. But the CP-conjugate rate difference is \(-4\operatorname{Im}(PQ^*)\operatorname{Im}(pq^*)\): a nonzero weak quartet needs a relative absorptive or controlled kinematic phase to become a rate asymmetry. Distinguishable independent pair events decohere and delete the term. See [the four-mode Pfaffian interference theorem](../0700-0799/734_the_four_mode_majorana_pfaffian_contains_the_quartet_interference_but_a_cp_rate_asymmetry_needs_a_relative_kinematic_phase.md).

That differential asymmetry can still vanish after inclusive integration. If a measure-preserving phase-space involution exchanges the two kinematic pairings \(P\leftrightarrow Q\), then \(\operatorname{Im}(PQ^*)\) is exchange odd: the pointwise CP density can be nonzero while its symmetric inclusive integral is exactly zero. A Breit–Wigner routing toy verifies the cancellation and shows that an exchange-odd signed moment, a half-space bin, or genuine mass/width/routing inequivalence can retain the signal. This is an observable-design theorem, not yet a Standard Model channel: the full amplitude must establish whether the involution survives sources, spins, cuts and identical-particle symmetrization. See [the pairing-exchange phase-space theorem](../0700-0799/735_pairing_exchange_can_cancel_the_inclusive_majorana_quartet_asymmetry_while_a_signed_differential_moment_restores_it.md).

The best exchange-odd weight is now fixed for any specified local differential model. If \(f=f_0+\epsilon f_1\), Cauchy–Schwarz bounds the weighted squared signal-to-noise by \(\int f_1^2/f_0\), with equality only for the likelihood score \(w_{\rm opt}\propto f_1/f_0\). Hence an exchange-symmetric inclusive rate can contain zero CP response while the fully differential distribution retains positive Fisher information. In the routed toy a sign weight retains about 92.9% of that information, but detector response and nuisance parameters require the score of the observed likelihood and projection onto the nuisance-orthogonal subspace. See [the optimal exchange-odd score theorem](../0700-0799/736_the_optimal_exchange_odd_observable_is_the_cp_score_and_recovers_all_local_information_lost_by_the_inclusive_rate.md).

Spin introduces a second, logically prior projection. For path operators \(A,B\) and spin density/analyzer \(\rho\), the quartet cross term is multiplied by \(C_\rho=\operatorname{Tr}(\rho AB^\dagger)\), and the CP difference is \(-4\operatorname{Im}(PQ^*C_\rho)\operatorname{Im}(pq^*)\). A vanishing unpolarized trace can be recoverable by polarization when \(AB^\dagger\) is nonzero but traceless; if \(AB^\dagger=0\), the paths are operator-orthogonal and no analyzer or optimal weight can restore the interference. A two-level certificate verifies both cases. The remaining physical task is to derive these operators from a gauge-complete LNV amplitude and transform spin preparations correctly under CP. See [the spin-survival and operator-orthogonality theorem](../0700-0799/737_an_unpolarized_spin_trace_can_erase_the_majorana_quartet_interference_while_polarization_restores_it_unless_the_paths_are_operator_orthogonal.md).

Nuisance profiling adds a third projection and now has an exact criterion. The efficient CP score is the raw score minus its Fisher-orthogonal projection onto the nuisance-score span, and its information is the corresponding Schur complement. Exchange-even normalization and width nuisances are exactly orthogonal to the odd CP score in the symmetric routed toy, but one odd acceptance tilt leaves only about 26.7% of the raw information. If an unconstrained nuisance tangent equals the CP tangent, the efficient information is exactly zero; a second differently oriented channel or external calibration is then necessary. See [the efficient-score identifiability theorem](../0700-0799/738_the_efficient_cp_score_is_the_nuisance_orthogonal_jordan_tangent_and_its_schur_complement_is_the_identifiability_test.md).

Detector folding is a preceding positive Markov compression and obeys a rigorous data-processing bound. For a parameter-independent response, the observed score is \(s_{\rm obs}(y)=\mathbb E[s_{\rm latent}(X)\mid Y=y]\), hence \(I_{\rm obs}\le I_{\rm latent}\), with loss equal to the expected conditional variance of the latent score. Gaussian smearing continuously contracts the routed-toy information, while complete mixing of pairing-exchanged orientations drives it to zero. Thus reconstructible orientation is a mathematical requirement: neither an optimal weight nor nuisance profiling can recover a score erased by the observation map. See [the detector-folding Fisher-contraction theorem](../0700-0799/739_detector_folding_is_a_positive_markov_compression_that_can_only_contract_cp_fisher_information.md).

An exact single-face nuisance degeneracy can nevertheless be broken by a controlled second face. For score pairs \((s_{CP},s_A)=(g,g)\) and \((g,-g)\), each channel alone has rank-one Fisher geometry and zero profiled CP information, while their joint determinant is \(4I_g^2L_+L_-\). The joint efficient information is \(4I_gL_+L_-/(L_++L_-)\), and balanced exposure retains all joint raw CP information. This establishes a canonical even/odd direct-sum gluing rule for measurements, but not its physical realization: the amplitude and apparatus must prove that the nuisance reverses while the CP score remains aligned. See [the reversed-measurement-face theorem](../0700-0799/740_two_reversed_measurement_faces_can_break_an_exact_single_face_cp_acceptance_degeneracy.md).

Imperfect reversal has an exact stability law. For the second-face nuisance score \(-\kappa g\), \(\det I=I_g^2L_+L_-(1+\kappa)^2\) and \(I_{CP}^{\rm eff}=I_gL_+L_-(1+\kappa)^2/(L_++\kappa^2L_-)\). Only \(\kappa=-1\)—identical score rows—is exactly singular, but the condition number diverges as that boundary is approached. In the certificate, \(\kappa=-0.9\) is formally full rank yet retains only about 0.28% of raw CP information with condition number about 1450. The relevant object is therefore the calibrated observed score map, not the nominal hardware reversal. See [the imperfect-reversal conditioning theorem](../0700-0799/741_imperfect_face_reversal_remains_identifying_except_at_the_same_sign_boundary_but_can_be_arbitrarily_ill_conditioned.md).

Orientation tagging contributes a separate exact contraction. A binary tag with fidelity \(r\) has odd-mode dilution \(D=2r-1\), so the observed odd score is multiplied by \(D\) and its Fisher information by \(D^2\). A 90% tag therefore retains 64%, while a random tag destroys all orientation information; a perfectly anticorrelated but calibrated tag is fully invertible. Because the leading signal depends on the product \(D\epsilon_{CP}\), dilution must be externally calibrated or jointly constrained by a control channel. See [the binary orientation-tag dilution theorem](../0700-0799/742_a_binary_pairing_orientation_tag_retains_the_square_of_its_dilution_in_cp_fisher_information.md).

The general many-face criterion is now a rank theorem. In the common-shape model with coefficient matrix \(A=[c\;N]\) and exposure matrix \(W\), the Fisher matrix is \(I_gA^TWA\) and the efficient CP information is \(I_gc^TW(I-P_N^W)c\). It is positive exactly when the CP face column lies outside the nuisance column span. With \(k\) independent nuisances, at least \(k+1\) suitably oriented faces are generically required; channel count alone is irrelevant if the observed score rank does not grow. The determinant is a squared Gram volume, generalizing the two-face area, while the smallest singular value controls stability. See [the multi-face design-rank theorem](../0700-0799/743_multiface_cp_identifiability_is_a_design_matrix_rank_problem_and_requires_a_direction_outside_the_nuisance_span.md).

For one physical and one nuisance score, the Fisher Gram matrix is exactly a positive \(H_2(\mathbb R)\) element. Its determinant is both the Lorentz norm \(t^2-x^2-y^2\) and the nuisance norm times the efficient physical information. Linear dependence lies on the light cone; identifiability lies in its interior. But this exact bridge has group \(SL(2,\mathbb R)\cong Spin_0(2,1)\), not \(Spin_0(2,3)\). The three-coordinate carrier cannot become the five-dimensional target by relabeling two extra statistics; a genuine \((2,3)\) module and intertwiner are required. See [the Fisher-Gram \(H_2(\mathbb R)\) group-boundary theorem](../0700-0799/744_the_two_score_fisher_gram_is_exactly_an_h2r_lorentz_cone_but_its_spin_group_is_spin21_not_spin23.md).

The genuine five-dimensional target is now constructed explicitly. For a symplectic four-space \((V,\omega)\), the primitive two-forms \(\Lambda_0^2V^*=\omega^\perp\subset\Lambda^2V^*\) have dimension five and wedge-pairing signature \((2,3)\). An explicit basis has Gram matrix \(2\operatorname{diag}(1,1,-1,-1,-1)\). All ten generators \(X=-JS\), \(S=S^T\), induce linearly independent \(5\times5\) transformations satisfying \(R_X^TG+GR_X=0\). This realizes the standard route \(Sp(4,\mathbb R)\cong Spin_0(2,3)\). It does not yet prove that Jordan-triple recursion supplies \((V,\omega)\); that antecedent is now the isolated central gap. See [the primitive-two-form \(Spin(2,3)\) construction](../0700-0799/745_the_primitive_two_forms_of_a_symplectic_four_space_give_the_genuine_five_dimensional_spin23_module.md).

The recurring \(H_2(\mathbb R)\) cone embeds as a signature-\((1,2)\) section of that module, with a complementary signature-\((1,1)\) plane. Its connected setwise stabilizer is locally \(SO_0(1,2)\times SO_0(1,1)\), dimension four, while its pointwise stabilizer is the one-dimensional complementary boost. Consequently such carrier sections form a six-dimensional \(SO_0(2,3)\) orbit. The cone therefore does not canonically choose its own ambient embedding: an internal CP/odd-axis selector and an ambient-section selector are separate problems. A genuine Jordan lift must either select this six-parameter orbit or generate the six carrier/complement mixing directions. See [the carrier-section stabilizer theorem](../0700-0799/746_an_h2r_carrier_embeds_as_a_signature12_section_of_the_spin23_module_but_leaves_a_six_parameter_embedding_degeneracy.md).

Those six directions are now explicit in both representations. Relative to carrier \(W=\langle T_1,X_1,X_2\rangle\) and complement \(W^\perp=\langle T_2,X_3\rangle\), the mixing sector contains three compact rotations and three noncompact boosts. Each has a concrete lift \(X=-JS\) in \(\mathfrak{sp}(4,\mathbb R)\). Together with \(\mathfrak h=\mathfrak{so}(1,2)\oplus\mathfrak{so}(1,1)\), they satisfy the symmetric-pair brackets \([h,h]\subset h\), \([h,m]\subset m\), \([m,m]\subset h\). Candidate Jordan triple derivations can therefore be tested by symplecticity, projection onto these six matrices, and ten-dimensional Lie closure. See [the explicit six-generator closure test](../0700-0799/747_the_six_missing_spin23_generators_are_three_rotations_and_three_boosts_with_explicit_sp4_lifts.md).

A cross-branch representation audit corrects the interpretation of that test. The declared threshold family in documents 653–654 already reconstructs the full \(3+4+3\) TKK basis, and this basis induces rank ten on primitive two-forms, with rank-four stabilizer and rank-six mixing projections relative to the chosen linear section. Moreover, the intrinsic conformal embedding of \(H_2(\mathbb R)\) is the null ray \(\iota(x)=(1,-q(x)/2,x)\), not a canonical linear three-plane; its ambient pairing gives \(-q(x-y)/2\). The linear-section \(4+6\) split is valid but choice-dependent, whereas the TKK \(3+4+3\) grading is intrinsic. The remaining gap is therefore physical covariance and source selection, not algebraic generator count. See [the TKK/primitive representation reconciliation](../0700-0799/748_the_tkk_algebra_already_induces_all_spin23_directions_and_the_jordan_carrier_embeds_projectively_in_the_null_cone.md).

Imposing exact \(B-L\) Ward normalization makes that physical reduction explicit in the ambient representation. The surviving algebra has TKK grade content \((2,2,0)\): two translations, two structure generators and no special-conformal wing. Its primitive-form action preserves a maximal totally isotropic two-plane and has derived-series dimensions \(4\to3\to1\to0\). It is therefore a solvable affine flag algebra, not full \(Spin(2,3)\). The full group can survive only as a transformation of Ward data or between theories unless the current protection is broken and all remaining gates pass. See [the Ward null-plane classification](../0700-0799/749_the_bl_ward_compatible_algebra_preserves_a_maximal_null_plane_and_is_affine_not_full_spin23.md).

Adding fixed UV data sharpens the before/after-Majorana comparison. Ward plus one fixed positive UV slope leaves only two abelian contact translations with grades \((2,0,0)\), preserving a maximal null plane; fixed contacts kill them. Removing continuous \(B-L\) while retaining one UV tensor enlarges the remnant to grades \((3,1,0)\), isomorphic to \(\mathbb R\oplus\mathfrak e(2)\), and reduces the invariant geometry to one null line. It still contains no dilation, full Lorentz algebra or special-conformal wing. Fixed contacts leave only the compact rotation, and two generic UV tensors kill that. See [the Majorana fixed-data flag comparison](../0700-0799/750_majorana_breaking_enlarges_the_fixed_data_remnant_from_a_null_plane_translation_algebra_to_a_null_line_euclidean_algebra_not_spin23.md).

Retaining the broken-current sector dynamically opens a different carrier fork rather than restoring the same group. Exact Nambu support closure is \(H_3(\mathbb C)\) with conformal algebra \(\mathfrak{su}(3,3)\). A heavy third Peirce face gives controlled Feshbach reduction only to \(H_2(\mathbb C)\); the remaining imaginary corner direction needs an independent real-structure selector. Direct conjugation averaging is not completely positive on one complex sector, but conjugate topology doubling, exchange twirling and topology superselection give a completely positive encoded realization. Thus \(Spin(2,3)\) returns only through two separately justified selectors, and the projected null-line regime must not be conflated with the dynamic \(H_3(\mathbb C)\) regime. See [the broken-current carrier-fork synthesis](../0700-0799/751_adjoining_the_majorana_breaking_channel_creates_an_h3c_carrier_fork_and_spin23_returns_only_after_two_independent_selectors.md).

The two-stage selector now has a quantitative control budget. If \(\ell\) is heavy-face population, \(c\) residual topology coherence and \(\epsilon\) exchange-twirl bias, then \(\|\Phi_{\rm approx}(\rho)-\Phi_{\rm ideal}(\rho)\|_1\le2\sqrt\ell+|c|+|\epsilon|\). For an isolated low-energy state, \(\sqrt\ell\le\|w\|/|\Delta-E|\). An executable doubled-carrier model verifies the bound over a gap sweep and shows the error approaching a topology-selection floor. Large \(\Delta\) therefore cannot compensate for weak superselection or exchange miscalibration. See [the finite selector error budget](../0700-0799/752_the_two_stage_majorana_selector_has_an_additive_finite_gap_coherence_and_exchange_error_budget.md).

The cubic-norm residue is also exact. For \(X=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&w\\w^\dagger&c\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in H_3(\mathbb C)\), \(\det X=c\det A-w^\dagger\operatorname{adj}(A)w=c\det(A-ww^\dagger/c)\). Hence the \(H_2\) determinant is the outward normal derivative of the cubic on the rank-two Peirce face and the normalized finite-gap Schur determinant. The cubic itself vanishes on the face; the residue is quadratic, not automatically an anomaly or logical cubic phase. This supplies a precise version of “the cubic preserves local consistency” while keeping the Prakash–Ramya comparison typed. See [the cubic-to-quadratic residue theorem](../0700-0799/753_the_h2_determinant_is_the_exact_normal_and_schur_residue_of_the_h3c_cubic_norm.md).

The exceptional version sharpens the boundary rather than removing it. For the Albert algebra \(H_3(\mathbb O)\), the normal derivative of the cubic norm at a rank-two Peirce face is exactly the \(H_2(\mathbb O)\) determinant \(\alpha\beta-|z|^2\). In trace/traceless coordinates this has signature \((1,9)\), so the first exceptional descent yields a ten-dimensional Lorentz carrier, not the \(H_2(\mathbb R)\) cone and not \(Spin_0(2,3)\). Restricting the octonionic response coordinate \(z\) to its real scalar line does recover \(H_2(\mathbb R)\), but suppressing the seven imaginary directions is a second selector whose covariance, positivity and physical meaning must be proved. See [the Albert normal-residue boundary](../0700-0799/754_the_albert_cubic_normal_residue_is_the_h2o_lorentz_norm_not_directly_the_spin23_h2r_norm.md).

A source-version re-entry confirms that arXiv:2610.21879v1 is already integrated rather than a new input. Its exact contribution is the integral triorthogonal charge lattice, the \(\nu^c\) puncture, the universal \([[15,1,3]]_p\) family and the Dirac-paired/weak-linker decomposition. None of those results supplies the symplectic four-carrier or identifies the anomaly cubic with the Jordan determinant cubic. The live bridge is an explicit typed intertwiner from code/logical data to a nondegenerate alternating carrier, after which primitive two-forms give the established \((2,3)\) module. See [the arXiv v1 re-entry and coverage audit](../0700-0799/755_arxiv_2610_21879v1_reentry_audit_and_current_frontier_map.md).

The most direct \(S_5\)-equivariant version of that bridge is now ruled out. The five charge coordinates split as the invariant line plus the four-dimensional sum-zero standard module, but the latter has no \(S_5\)-invariant alternating form. Worse, puncturing \(\nu^c\) leaves the rank-one cubic \(\tau_{15}(x,y,z)=-(\sum x)(\sum y)(\sum z)\), which vanishes whenever any argument is in the standard four-module. Thus neither symmetry nor cubic polarization pairs the dimensionally tempting four-space. Extra symmetry breaking, doubling, a central extension or logical-commutator data is mandatory. See [the \(S_5\) puncture-cubic symplectic no-go](../0700-0799/756_the_s5_puncture_cubic_lives_on_the_trivial_line_and_cannot_symplectically_pair_the_standard_four_module.md).

The subgroup escape has now been classified exhaustively. Of the 19 conjugacy classes of subgroups of \(S_5\), only the trivial group, a double-transposition \(C_2\), a three-cycle \(C_3\), and a five-cycle \(C_5\) permit a nondegenerate invariant alternating form on the standard four-module. The natural \(S_3\times S_2\) stabilizer of the color/weak \(3+2\) partition has none. Each nontrivial survivor leaves a two-dimensional family of invariant forms, so symmetry reduction permits but does not select the carrier. See [the exhaustive subgroup symplectic classification](../0700-0799/757_only_four_subgroup_types_of_s5_make_the_standard_four_module_symplectic_and_the_color_weak_stabilizer_is_not_one.md).

The actual Higgs-selected crossed-octonion partition has now been inserted into that classification. On all sixteen five-bit weights it is simply the sign split \(q_4=\pm1\), so its full permutation stabilizer is \(S_4\) fixing the distinguished weak coordinate. Intersecting with the physical color/weak block stabilizer gives color \(S_3\). The former has no invariant two-form on the standard four-module; the latter has one but it is rank-two degenerate. Only a further orientation choice \(S_3\to A_3\cong C_3\) reaches a symplectic subgroup type, and it still leaves two area coefficients. See [the Higgs-partition stabilizer theorem](../0700-0799/758_the_higgs_crossed_octonion_partition_reduces_s5_to_s4_or_physical_s3_but_still_does_not_select_a_symplectic_four_carrier.md).

The oriented-color escape is now explicit. The \(C_3\)-invariant two-forms are \(J(a,b)=aJ_c+bJ_f\), pairing the color-difference and complementary fixed planes, with \(\operatorname{Pf}J=3ab\). A bare color reflection reverses only \(J_c\) and therefore fails even as an anti-symplectic symmetry. Pairing it with the weak-coordinate swap reverses both areas, producing an order-six signed covariance with three symplectic and three anti-symplectic elements. This discrete extension acts orthogonally on primitive two-forms, but it leaves the ratio \(b/a\) free and exposes an index-three obstruction to integral unimodularity. See [the oriented-color signed-symplectic theorem](../0700-0799/759_oriented_color_c3_gives_a_two_area_symplectic_family_and_color_reflection_becomes_antisymplectic_only_when_paired_with_weak_exchange.md).

For the equal-area form, that index-three obstruction has a unique signed-covariant resolution. Its discriminant group is \((\mathbb Z/3)^2\), producing four self-dual index-three overlattices. Exactly one is stable under both the color cycle and paired color/weak reflection: adjoining the color-average vector \((b_1+b_2+b_3)/3\). This yields an integral unimodular symplectic four-lattice and hence an arithmetic \(Sp(4)\) carrier. The theorem is conditional on equal areas; neither cyclic nor signed covariance fixes their ratio. See [the color-average unimodular completion theorem](../0700-0799/760_signed_color_weak_covariance_uniquely_selects_the_color_average_index_three_unimodular_overlattice_conditional_on_equal_areas.md).

That arithmetic carrier has now been put in canonical form. An explicit integral basis sends it to \((\mathbb Z^4,J_0)\), and its primitive two-form lattice is \(U\oplus U\oplus\langle-2\rangle\), with signature \((2,3)\) and determinant \(-2\). The color cycle induces an integral determinant-one isometry, while the paired anti-symplectic reflection induces a determinant-minus-one isometry. Thus the discrete branch lands exactly in a signed integral \(O(2,3)\) action, with parity outside the connected \(Spin_0(2,3)\) component. See [the canonical integral carrier and primitive-lattice theorem](../0700-0799/761_the_color_average_completion_has_canonical_sp4z_form_and_primitive_lattice_u_plus_u_plus_minus2_with_signed_o23_action.md).

Modulo three, the completed carrier becomes a nondegenerate four-dimensional symplectic space, abstractly identical to the Pauli phase space of two logical qutrits; its ambient group is \(Sp(4,3)\) of order 51840. The color cycle becomes an order-three unipotent, while the paired reflection remains anti-symplectic. This creates an exact finite-type bridge to the two-puncture code branch, but not a canonical map: a field-labelled logical basis and compatibility with the anomaly cubic remain absent. See [the mod-three carrier/code comparison](../0700-0799/762_modulo_three_the_color_average_carrier_is_an_abstract_two_qutrit_pauli_phase_space_but_no_field_labelled_intertwiner_is_yet_selected.md).

Color equivariance closes the most natural choice of that map. The physical color three-cycle fixes both \((\nu^c,e^c)\) punctures and therefore acts identically on the full code logical Pauli quotient. On the arithmetic carrier it is a nontrivial unipotent with three-dimensional fixed space. Any equivariant linear map has rank at most three, so no invertible color-equivariant identification exists. The abstract \(Sp(4,3)\) match survives, but a physical bridge must use non-Weyl encoded operations, puncture motion, a different boundary or an emergent response-level color action. See [the color-cycle intertwiner no-go](../0700-0799/763_the_physical_color_cycle_acts_trivially_on_two_puncture_code_logicals_but_unipotently_on_the_arithmetic_carrier_so_no_equivariant_identification_exists.md).

Pure Weyl puncture motion does not supply that exit. The exhaustive \(D_5\) groupoid transports ordered logical evaluations identically and adds at most an order-two coordinate swap for unordered boundaries. The carrier color action is order three with rank-one nilpotent deviation from identity. Order and \(\operatorname{rank}(M-I)\) rule out conjugacy in \(GL(4,3)\), hence also in \(Sp(4,3)\), for every Weyl-related puncture pair. A non-Weyl Clifford, duality, surgery or emergent response action is necessary. See [the puncture-motion conjugacy no-go](../0700-0799/764_pure_d5_weyl_puncture_motion_has_only_identity_or_swap_transport_and_cannot_realize_the_order_three_carrier_color_cycle.md).

The required non-Weyl matrix nevertheless has a minimal exact realization in the genus-two \(A_2\) Weil carrier: \(R_{\rm car}=T_2^{-1}F_2^{-1}\), an inverse quadratic twist followed by inverse Fourier motion on the second handle. It fixes the first logical pair and uses no mutual sum-cycle gate. This solves encoded realizability but not physical identification: microscopic color permutation remains trivial on code logicals, while the Weil word is a response/mapping-class operation. Because it is one-handle local, it also cannot select the equal-area relation. See [the one-handle Weil-word theorem](../0700-0799/765_the_carrier_color_unipotent_is_exactly_a_one_handle_inverse_twist_fourier_word_not_a_microscopic_color_permutation_or_mutual_gate.md).

The equal-area antecedent now has an exact conditional selector. In the completed lattice, exchanging the color-average/weak handle with the color-difference handle sends \(J(a,b)\) to \(J(b,a)\). Requiring the exchange to be symplectic forces \(a=b\); requiring it to be anti-symplectic forces \(a=-b\). The swap is integral only after the color-average index-three completion and is not an ordinary \(S_5\) permutation. Thus the arithmetic chain becomes unconditional only if a physical Furey, ribbon, code-boundary or response reciprocity realizes this handle exchange. See [the handle-exchange equal-area theorem](../0700-0799/766_integral_handle_exchange_forces_equal_or_opposite_carrier_areas_but_is_not_a_microscopic_s5_symmetry.md).

The actual sewn labels obstruct a microscopic realization. The handles \(C=(u,e)\) and \(D=(d,\nu)\) are the diagonals of the channel-by-color/lepton square. Four untyped permutations exchange them, but none preserves both physical typings; exact charge typing leaves only the identity. Thus the ribbon topology does not itself prove equal areas. A valid selector must come from restored left-right symmetry, a multiplicity-correct Pati-Salam completion, an emergent equality of response Gram forms, or an encoded duality with a controlled matter intertwiner. See [the sewn Furey handle-exchange obstruction](../0700-0799/767_the_sewn_furey_ribbon_has_untyped_handle_exchange_but_no_standard_model_typed_exchange_between_c_and_d.md).

The minimal enlarged gauge envelope is now identified. The Standard Model realizes channel flip only on the two left-handed weak doublets; right-handed up/down and neutrino/electron fields carry unequal hypercharges. Adding \(SU(2)_R\) and the already independently selected \(\nu_R\) completes the two right doublets, and the simultaneous nontrivial Weyl elements of \(SU(2)_L\times SU(2)_R\) exchange \(C\) and \(D\). Full Pati-Salam \(SU(4)\) is optional for this step. The remaining stop-everything test is dynamical: classify the left-right-invariant quadratic response and determine whether it has one handle coefficient or two. See [the left-right handle-exchange envelope](../0700-0799/768_left_right_symmetry_is_the_minimal_gauge_envelope_of_the_furey_handle_exchange_but_equal_area_requires_an_invariant_response_phase.md).

On the already selected block-local two-area carrier, that response test is positive but conditional on the missing intertwiner. If the simultaneous left-right Weyl element acts as the integral handle swap, invariance forces \(a=b\) in the symmetric phase. For an exchange-odd breaking spurion \(s\), covariance gives \(b(s)=a(-s)\): the mean area is even and the splitting is odd, so linear broken-phase splitting is generic. The new central arrow is an explicit matter-to-carrier map that both intertwines these actions and excludes additional mixed alternating blocks. See [the left-right response and odd-spurion splitting theorem](../0700-0799/769_an_intertwined_left_right_response_forces_equal_handle_areas_in_the_symmetric_phase_but_allows_odd_spurion_splitting.md).

The mixed-block loophole is now closed algebraically. Starting from all six alternating forms, simultaneous oriented-color symplectic covariance, paired color/weak anti-symplectic covariance and handle-exchange symplectic covariance impose a rank-five system. The unique surviving line is \(\mathbb R^\times(J_c+J_f)\), with Pfaffian three. Thus the full symplectic form is selected up to scale if one physical response quotient realizes the three actions. The programme's live burden is no longer form selection but construction—or obstruction—of that common Furey/left-right intertwiner. See [the unique full symplectic-line theorem](../0700-0799/770_oriented_color_signed_reflection_and_left_right_handle_exchange_select_a_unique_full_symplectic_line_with_no_mixed_block_loophole.md).

The naive common matter intertwiner is now ruled out. Although the three carrier selectors have orders three, two and two, their word \(W=(PHR)^2\) obeys \(W^2-3W+I=0\) and has reciprocal eigenvalues \((3\pm\sqrt5)/2\), so the generated carrier group is infinite and hyperbolic. No invertible intertwiner from a positive-definite unitary matter representation can preserve that spectrum. The combined selector must therefore arise through non-unitary response or transfer dynamics, an indefinite/Krein carrier with a positive physical projection, or mapping-class/encoded duality. See [the hyperbolic selector-group no-go](../0700-0799/771_the_three_carrier_selectors_generate_a_hyperbolic_infinite_order_word_so_no_positive_unitary_matter_intertwiner_can_realize_them_all.md).

The hyperbolic word itself is reducible by handle. In the basis \((p_1,q_1,p_2,q_2)\), it is the direct sum of two trace-three \(SL(2,\mathbb Z)\) matrices, with stretch \(\varphi^2\). Its stable and unstable two-planes are Lagrangian, but it contains no mutual-handle mixing and admits a reducible lift by independent torus Anosov maps. Homology alone neither proves nor excludes a pseudo-Anosov lift after Torelli modification. The response branch now splits cleanly between independent-handle transfer and a genuinely coupled word containing the mutual transvection. See [the doubled trace-three handle analysis](../0700-0799/772_the_hyperbolic_selector_word_is_a_reducible_pair_of_trace_three_handle_maps_with_golden_ratio_squared_stretch.md).

The first genuinely coupled word is now explicit. In the positive alphabet generated by oriented color \(R\), signed reflection \(P\), handle exchange \(H\) and primitive mutual shear \(U\), the shortest symplectic word with irreducible non-cyclotomic reciprocal quartic is \(M=U^2HR\). Its polynomial \(x^4+2x^3+x^2+2x+1\) destroys every rational handle splitting and has one real reciprocal pair plus one unit-circle pair. Crucially, \(U^2\bmod3\) is exactly the unique two-qutrit mutual shear derived from the sum-cycle twist. This supplies a sharp algebraic realization of the third relationship while retaining the boundary that irreducible homology is not a proof of pseudo-Anosov topology. See [the shortest coupled mutual-shear word](../0700-0799/773_the_shortest_coupled_selector_word_uses_the_unique_qutrit_mutual_shear_and_has_an_irreducible_partially_hyperbolic_quartic.md).

Full hyperbolicity first appears at length eight in the same positive alphabet: \(N=(UPHR)^2\), with irreducible polynomial \(x^4-7x^3+13x^2-7x+1\). Both reduced roots \((7\pm\sqrt5)/2\) exceed two, giving two real carrier expansion rates. On primitive two-forms the spectrum is \(\lambda_1\lambda_2,\lambda_1/\lambda_2,1,\lambda_2/\lambda_1,(\lambda_1\lambda_2)^{-1}\), an explicit integral \(SO(2,3)\) action with two boost pairs and one fixed line. This is a concrete noncompact endpoint, not yet a derived physical transfer law. See [the fully hyperbolic coupled Spin(2,3) word](../0700-0799/774_the_shortest_fully_hyperbolic_coupled_word_has_an_irreducible_quartic_and_a_one_plus_two_reciprocal_pair_spin23_spectrum.md).

The fixed primitive line is now geometrically typed. In a diagonal \((2,3)\) basis it is generated by \((1,1,2,-1,0)\) and has norm \(-10\). The word therefore fixes a non-null negative axis, not a Ward-like null flag. Its orthogonal complement has signature \((2,2)\) and splits into two Lorentzian planes carrying the reciprocal boost pairs. This spectral flag does not itself choose the earlier \((1,2)\) observable section or identify the fixed axis with a physical Casimir; those remain tasks for the response map. See [the fixed-axis and two-boost-plane theorem](../0700-0799/775_the_fully_hyperbolic_selector_fixes_a_negative_norm_primitive_axis_and_splits_its_orthogonal_complement_into_two_boost_planes.md).

The first passivity boundary is also exact. The fully hyperbolic word is symplectic but not orthogonal; its singular values are approximately \(4.81981,2.12235,0.471176,0.207477\), requiring Bloch-Messiah squeezings \(1.57273\) and \(0.752525\) if interpreted as a direct canonical Gaussian step. It can nevertheless be a passive spatial or boundary transfer matrix, because passive evanescent transfer need not preserve Euclidean amplitude norm even when scattering preserves flux. The anti-symplectic factors are orientation operations, not individual Hamiltonian time steps. See [the active-step/passive-transfer distinction](../0700-0799/776_the_fully_hyperbolic_selector_requires_active_squeezing_as_a_canonical_step_but_can_still_be_a_passive_flux_transfer_matrix.md).

The transfer-to-scattering conversion now exists explicitly. Complex polarization sends the real symplectic word to a Bogoliubov matrix preserving \(\Sigma=\operatorname{diag}(I_2,-I_2)\); solving the two-sided transfer relation produces a unitary four-channel scattering matrix. This proves compatibility with passive flux scattering, but only conditionally: the conjugate half must represent counterpropagating spatial channels. If it is instead a temporal Nambu doubling, the nonzero \(\beta\) block is active pair conversion. A source-derived boundary orientation and real structure are therefore the exact next gate. See [the pseudo-unitary-transfer/unitary-scattering theorem](../0700-0799/777_complex_polarization_turns_the_hyperbolic_symplectic_word_into_pseudo_unitary_transfer_and_unitary_scattering_but_the_spatial_real_structure_is_the_gate.md).

The required real structure has an exact Furey candidate. The completed survivor exchange is \(\sigma_xK\), squares to one, reverses the transfer flux metric and fixes the explicit Bogoliubov transfer matrix. On the real carrier it is \((q,p)\mapsto(q,-p)\): anti-symplectic, pointwise fixing the sewn meridian plane and reversing the longitude plane. It therefore supplies reciprocity but neither Fourier exchange nor longitude framing. The remaining physical gate is whether the survivor-topology pair really is the counterpropagating boundary pair; the gauge-conjugating Furey CAR involution must remain separate. See [the Furey spatial-flux real-structure match](../0700-0799/778_the_furey_survivor_exchange_has_exactly_the_spatial_flux_reversal_real_structure_but_preserves_the_sewn_meridian_plane.md).

The repeated binary structures are now fully typed. Handle, survivor topology, propagation orientation and Nambu conjugation are four distinct indices. On \(\mathbb C^2_H\otimes\mathbb C^2_S\otimes\mathbb C^2_R\), the survivor and propagation real structures jointly leave exactly four real dimensions; adding an independently constrained Nambu pair still leaves four. Thus no mirror doubling is forced. But \(\sigma_xK\) is gauge-equivariant on a \((+q,-q)\) Nambu pair and not on a same-charge pair for \(q\ne0\), so survivor/propagation locking must occur only after gauge-neutral response compression. See [the four-binary-index dimension and gauge ledger](../0700-0799/779_handle_survivor_propagation_and_nambu_are_four_distinct_binary_indices_whose_real_structures_can_leave_exactly_four_real_carrier_dimensions.md).

There is no generic analytic-passivity obstruction at the scattering level. Multiplying the symmetric unitary checkpoint by a normalized upper-half-plane Blaschke factor produces a reciprocal rational inner \(S(z)\) that reaches the exact checkpoint at finite real frequency, is strictly Schur in the upper half-plane and has a positive-real Cayley response. This proves causal passive interpolation exists in complex-envelope form. It does not derive the arithmetic matrix: the pole scale is free, the dressing is scalar and no residues are yet tied to Furey thresholds. See [the rational-inner positive-real embedding](../0700-0799/780_the_selected_unitary_scattering_checkpoint_embeds_in_a_reciprocal_rational_inner_response_with_positive_real_cayley_impedance.md).

Real-time frequency reality also admits an exact completion. Pairing \(S_+(z)\) with \(\overline{S_+(-\bar z)}\) and imposing a sideband-exchange antiunitary gives an analytic reciprocal response that is unitary on the real axis, contractive in the upper half-plane and satisfies the correct positive/negative-frequency relation. The doubled complex envelope does not add independent physical states after the reality constraint. The new sideband bit is nevertheless distinct from handle, survivor, propagation and Nambu indices. See [the real-time sideband completion](../0700-0799/781_a_sideband_exchange_real_structure_gives_the_complex_scattering_checkpoint_a_real_time_reciprocal_passive_completion.md).

One-point spectral interpretation is now ruled out as evidence. A lossless boundary checkpoint has a Hermitian Cayley-Herglotz value and is reproduced by a constant zero-pole response. Even one interior value \(A+iB\) can be matched at any arbitrarily chosen real pole using the positive residue \(R=B((m-x)^2+y^2)/y\) and a compensating Hermitian contact. Thus no Furey mass or threshold follows from the present checkpoint while contacts are free. Fixed UV data, multiple frequencies or derivatives and typed residue ranges are mandatory. See [the one-point residue non-identifiability theorem](../0700-0799/782_one_scattering_checkpoint_cannot_identify_furey_thresholds_or_positive_residues_when_hermitian_contact_terms_are_free.md).

The scalar causal embedding also fails field-local residue typing in the declared basis. Every Cayley response and residue is a rational function of \(S_0\) and therefore commutes with it. Because every off-diagonal entry of \(S_0\) is nonzero, its diagonal commutant is only \(\mathbb CI\); exhaustive coordinate-projector testing leaves only zero and identity. Scalar dressing therefore produces coherent relational spectral channels, not separate handle-, side- or field-coordinate residues. A typed model needs either a source-derived non-diagonal support intertwiner or genuinely matrix-valued frequency dependence. See [the scalar-residue locality no-go](../0700-0799/783_scalar_dressing_forces_coherent_relational_residue_channels_and_cannot_supply_nontrivial_coordinate_local_furey_residues.md).

Those coherent channels are now explicit. The checkpoint has four simple unit-circle eigenvalues and four rank-one projectors. Every projector has nonzero weight on all four left/right and \(C/D\) coordinates, hence touches both handles and both boundary sides. Nevertheless two channels are strongly \(C\)-biased and two strongly \(D\)-biased, with handle weights ranging from about 0.049 to 0.951. The scalar response is therefore genuinely relational but not uniformly mixed. These participation numbers are arithmetic-word data, not Standard Model mixing angles. See [the four relational scattering eigenchannels](../0700-0799/784_all_four_scattering_eigenchannels_are_rank_one_relational_superpositions_touching_both_handles_and_both_boundary_sides.md).

The fixed field-grid intertwiner is now ruled out. Projector-overlap matrices show that neither handle exchange nor antiunitary flux reversal permutes the four spectral lines; their row maxima remain strictly below one. Oriented color and handle exchange also have nonzero commutators with the fully hyperbolic carrier word. Thus the eigenchannel quartet is not an equivariant copy of \(u,d,e,\nu\). The surviving formulation is spurion covariance of a response family: selectors move between backgrounds, while a chosen broken-phase checkpoint has mixed dynamical eigenchannels. See [the spectral-quartet representation no-go](../0700-0799/785_the_scattering_eigenchannel_quartet_is_not_an_equivariant_permutation_copy_of_the_furey_udenu_grid.md).

That spurion family closes finitely. The integral group generated by oriented color and handle exchange has order eighteen and decomposes as \(C_3\times S_3\), with central generator \(Z=(HR)^2\). The fully hyperbolic checkpoint has trivial stabilizer, so its conjugacy orbit is a regular eighteen-point torsor. All orbit points retain the same irreducible polynomial and \(Spin(2,3)\) spectrum while their eigendirections move relative to the handle/color frame. The eighteen points are backgrounds, not particles or generations. See [the finite eighteen-background spurion orbit](../0700-0799/786_oriented_color_and_handle_exchange_generate_c3_times_s3_and_the_hyperbolic_checkpoint_has_a_regular_18_background_spurion_orbit.md).

Flux reversal does not quotient that torsor. The anti-symplectic involution \(K=\operatorname{diag}(I_2,-I_2)\) commutes with handle exchange but does not normalize the oriented-color generator. In fact \(W=(KR)^2\) contains the trace-three block \(\begin{psmallmatrix}1&-1\\-1&2\end{psmallmatrix}\), with polynomial \(x^2-3x+1\), so adjoining \(K\) makes both the selector group and the checkpoint conjugacy orbit infinite. The finite base must therefore remain the eighteen-point \(C_3\times S_3\) torsor, with flux reversal represented as an antiunitary real structure on the response fibers. See [the flux-reversal infinite-extension theorem](../0700-0799/787_flux_reversal_does_not_normalize_the_finite_selector_torsor_and_its_adjunction_generates_an_infinite_hyperbolic_extension.md).

The eighteen backgrounds now have an exact combinatorial factorization. Every group element and checkpoint conjugate has a unique normal form \(Z^a r^iH^e\), which identifies the regular torsor with one central three-phase, one of the three perfect matchings of \(\{u,d,e,\nu\}\), and a two-valued ordering of the paired handles. The \(S_3\) action on matching plus order is regular, not merely the ordinary three-point permutation action. This is an exact equivariant labelling, but only the matching \(((u,e),(d,\nu))\) is presently source-derived; the central phase is not yet proved to be mutual framing. See [the framed-pairing factorization](../0700-0799/788_the_18_background_torsor_is_exactly_three_framed_matter_pairings_times_a_central_three_phase_but_the_physical_source_map_is_open.md).

Standard-Model typing selects that sewn matching uniquely. It is the only one of the three perfect matchings for which every pair crosses both the up/down partition and the colored/singlet partition. Its unframed stabilizer inside the \(S_3\) factor is \(\{1,H\}\), leaving at most a six-point candidate stratum \(C_3\times C_2\) inside the eighteen-point atlas. “At most” is essential: \(H\) is not a microscopic typed symmetry and the central phase is still unsourced, so the physical background space may be smaller. See [the unique double-transversal theorem](../0700-0799/789_standard_model_typing_uniquely_selects_the_furey_double_transversal_pairing_and_leaves_at_most_a_six_point_selector_stratum.md).

The remaining central \(C_3\) is not the primitive mutual shear in disguise. Integrally, \(Z=(HR)^2\) has finite order while \(U\) is infinite unipotent. Modulo three they share order, rank and square-zero nilpotency type, yet exhaustive enumeration of all \(51{,}840\) elements of \(Sp(4,3)\) finds no conjugator from \(Z\) to \(U\) or \(U^{-1}\). Their centralizers have orders 216 and 108, respectively. The central coordinate must therefore remain a selector phase, not “mutual framing,” unless a non-invertible physical response functor supplies a different bridge. See [the central-phase/mutual-shear non-identification theorem](../0700-0799/790_the_central_selector_c3_is_not_the_primitive_mutual_shear_even_modulo_three_and_cannot_yet_be_called_framing.md).

Genuinely matrix-valued causal dressing exists without relaxing the analytic gates. Four coordinate-local normalized Potapov factors can be placed in a diagonal \(D(z)\) and coupled reciprocally as \(S(z)=D(z)S_0D(z)\). The result recovers the checkpoint, is symmetric and inner on the real axis, strictly Schur in the upper half-plane, has positive-real Cayley response and admits the same sideband reality completion. But every off-diagonal entry remains nonzero on the boundary, so its Cayley pole eigenspaces—and hence simple positive residue ranges—cannot be coordinate-local. Moreover, checkpoint normalization leaves all four pole scales free. See [the typed-Potapov compatibility and residue-locality theorem](../0700-0799/791_typed_coordinate_potapov_factors_preserve_the_checkpoint_passivity_reciprocity_and_reality_but_their_cayley_residues_remain_relational.md).

Using the checkpoint spectral projectors instead supplies a complete positive relational-residue model. The four projectors are real symmetric Hermitian rank-one supports; independent normalized Blaschke factors give \(S(z)=\sum_j\lambda_jf_j(z)^2P_j\). Each channel has two finite real Cayley poles with explicitly positive residue \(\rho_{j,m}P_j\), where \(\rho_{j,m}=(m^2+\gamma_j^2)/(2\gamma_j)\). The executable model therefore has eight positive rank-one residues and satisfies every analytic gate. It remains functional calculus in \(S_0\): the field-to-projector assignment and all four scales are unsourced. See [the explicit positive coherent-residue construction](../0700-0799/792_checkpoint_spectral_projectors_give_an_explicit_positive_rank_one_relational_residue_model_but_not_furey_typing_or_threshold_prediction.md).

The minimum data required to identify such residues is now exact when poles and UV contact are fixed. For \(n\) known distinct poles, \(n\) distinct off-pole real samples form an invertible Cauchy system and uniquely recover \(n\) unrestricted matrix residues; positivity then becomes a falsification test. With fewer samples, nontrivial null directions permit distinct positive residue families. If orthogonal coherent supports and their pole assignments are independently known, projection reduces the burden channel by channel, potentially to one sample for one pole per support. Unknown thresholds remain a nonlinear problem. See [the fixed-UV Cauchy-rank identifiability theorem](../0700-0799/793_fixed_uv_and_known_poles_turn_residue_recovery_into_a_cauchy_rank_problem_with_an_exact_minimum_data_theorem.md).

Unknown positive thresholds become identifiable from moments once the finite atomic order is fixed. In each coherent channel, \(2n\) moments \(\mu_0,\ldots,\mu_{2n-1}\) determine \(n\) distinct positive poles and weights through an invertible Hankel/Prony recurrence followed by a Vandermonde solve. Positive ordinary and shifted Hankel matrices, positive real roots and positive recovered weights provide independent falsification gates. The bound is sharp: two distinct positive two-atom measures explicitly share their first three moments but differ at the fourth. See [the positive-threshold moment-recovery theorem](../0700-0799/794_two_n_moments_generically_recover_n_positive_thresholds_and_weights_per_coherent_channel_while_two_n_minus_one_do_not.md).

Vacuum total stress cannot supply the four required moment sequences by itself. Conservation and Lorentz invariance leave at most the spin-two and spin-zero nonlocal form factors, so any linear observation of four relational channels has rank at most two and at least a two-dimensional hidden kernel; the conformal limit has rank at most one. Explicit distinct positive four-channel moment families give identical stress data at several moment orders. Universal gravitational coupling therefore enforces the correct aggregate Ward structure but is not four-channel tomography. See [the vacuum total-stress tomography no-go](../0700-0799/795_vacuum_total_stress_has_at_most_two_nonlocal_form_factor_moments_and_cannot_tomograph_four_relational_projector_channels.md).

The missing source rank can be supplied kinematically by the four Higgs-dressed Yukawa Gram composites. Their endpoint representations match exactly, making all four source channels gauge neutral. The \(u,d,e,\nu\) source matrix has rank four only after the \(\nu_R\) completion; the 15-state \(u,d,e\) branch has rank at most three. A hybrid using two stress aggregates and quark/lepton neutral contrasts is rank four precisely when the second aggregate has a different quark/lepton ratio from the color-multiplicity row. The source-to-projector map remains missing. See [the neutral Yukawa source-rank theorem](../0700-0799/796_four_higgs_dressed_yukawa_gram_sources_can_supply_rank_four_tomography_only_in_the_nu_right_completed_branch.md).

Positive unital order structure reduces the source-to-projector map to a finite problem. Both the four neutral source idempotents and the four spectral projectors generate ordered algebras isomorphic to \(\mathbb R^4\); an order-isomorphism must permute primitive idempotents, leaving exactly 24 assignments and 384 signed amplitude lifts. Every base assignment extends covariantly over the regular eighteen-background torsor, but the trivial stabilizer removes no choices. Covariance propagates a map rather than selecting it. See [the 24-map order-isomorphism theorem](../0700-0799/797_positive_unital_source_to_projector_maps_reduce_to_24_order_isomorphisms_but_regular_torsor_covariance_selects_none.md).

The actual participation data reduce those 24 assignments much further. The four projectors form a complete handle-by-side bias square, while \(u,d,e,\nu\) form the Furey handle-by-Higgs-channel square. Preserving named handle labels and mapping the channel bit to the side bit leaves exactly two assignments, related by global reversal \(up\leftrightarrow left/right\). At the selected checkpoint the smallest bias margin is \(0.158\). See [the selected-background bias-square match](../0700-0799/798_the_checkpoint_projectors_form_a_handle_by_side_bias_square_matching_the_furey_handle_by_channel_square_up_to_one_global_reversal.md).

That bias square is not confined to one checkpoint. Exact enumeration of all eighteen selector backgrounds, followed by 72 spectral-projector reconstructions, finds one projector in every \(C/D\times L/R\) cell at every background. The smallest orbit-wide margin is approximately \(0.04195\). Under the fixed polarization and port convention, the four bias cells globally label the discrete eigenbundle and the two source assignments extend across the entire torsor. The remaining global reversal is now a concrete causal-orientation question. See [the orbit-wide bias-square theorem](../0700-0799/799_the_handle_by_side_bias_square_survives_all_18_selector_backgrounds_and_globally_labels_the_discrete_eigenbundle.md).

Flux reversal types that final ambiguity exactly. The antiunitary \(XK\) preserves handle participation and complements left participation, so it sends \(CL\leftrightarrow CR\) and \(DL\leftrightarrow DR\). It exchanges the two global Furey-to-projector assignments rather than fixing either one. The remaining \(\mathbb Z_2\) is therefore an orientation torsor whose representative can be selected only by retarded/advanced, incoming/outgoing, time-orientation or oriented-sewing data. See [the flux-orientation torsor theorem](../0800-0899/800_flux_reversal_exchanges_the_two_global_furey_to_projector_assignments_so_the_last_bit_is_an_orientation_torsor.md).

The retarded/advanced prescription does not by itself point that torsor. Upper- and lower-boundary values of one Hermitian positive spectral measure share the same pole projectors and differ only in the absorptive boundary sign; their handle/side bias labels are identical. Spatial port reversal \(XK\), not the sign of \(i0\), complements side participation. A physical radiation condition must correlate causal and spatial orientations as extra boundary data. See [the retarded/advanced orientation no-go](../0800-0899/801_retarded_and_advanced_boundary_values_share_the_same_projector_residues_so_causality_alone_does_not_select_the_flux_orientation_torsor.md).

An orientation-sensitive observable nevertheless exists. With propagation grading \(\Gamma_R=\operatorname{diag}(1,1,-1,-1)\) and Higgs-channel signs \(\chi_{u,\nu}=+1,\chi_{d,e}=-1\), the cross-correlation \(\mathcal I_{\rm orient}=\sum_f\chi_f\operatorname{Tr}(P_{\Phi(f)}\Gamma_R)\) is approximately \(+2.43981\) for the up-to-left map and its negative for the reversed map. It remains nonzero on all eighteen backgrounds, with orbit-wide magnitude between approximately \(0.879\) and \(2.842\). See [the channel-times-flux orientation observable](../0800-0899/802_a_higgs_channel_times_flux_imbalance_cross_correlation_is_orientation_odd_and_distinguishes_the_two_global_source_maps.md).

Positive channel weights do not weaken the sign. Dividing the weighted cross-correlation by the corresponding positive absolute-flux sum gives an exact index \(\nu_{\rm orient}=\pm1\), independent of arbitrary nonnegative diagonal residue strengths. Flux reversal changes its sign. Exact quantization assumes the bias-square assignment and absence of coherent cross-channel contamination, turning mixing-induced departures from unity into the next error-budget target. See [the positive-weight normalized orientation index](../0800-0899/803_positive_channel_weights_cancel_from_a_normalized_orientation_index_leaving_an_exact_plus_or_minus_one_flux_bit.md).

For dephased positive source mixing, that error budget is exact. Splitting absolute flux into correctly and incorrectly aligned parts \(A,B\) gives \(\nu_{\rm orient}=(A-B)/(A+B)=1-2\epsilon\), where \(\epsilon=B/(A+B)\). The orientation sign is stable precisely for \(\epsilon<1/2\), vanishes at one half and reverses above it. The threshold is sharp and is controlled by flux-weighted rather than raw probability leakage. Coherent amplitude interference remains a separate operator-norm problem. See [the stochastic orientation error-budget theorem](../0800-0899/804_dephased_source_mixing_changes_the_orientation_index_by_exactly_twice_the_wrong_side_flux_fraction_with_a_sharp_50_percent_sign_threshold.md).

Coherent unitary mixing now has its own orbit-wide certificate. For flux grading \(\Gamma_R\), the expectation changes by at most \(2\|W-I\|_{\rm op}\). The minimum side-bias gap across all backgrounds therefore guarantees every orientation sign when \(\|W-I\|_{\rm op}<0.04194695\). The exact worst-channel plane rotation reaches zero only at \(0.04199323\), making the simple bound nearly sharp. See [the coherent unitary orientation bound](../0800-0899/805_coherent_unitary_source_mixing_preserves_the_orientation_below_an_orbit_wide_operator_norm_threshold_with_a_sharp_rotation_boundary.md).

A polar decomposition extends the certificate to general invertible source maps. After optimal scalar normalization, \(A=WU\) obeys \(\|cA-I\|\le r+(1+r)\eta\), with \(r=(\kappa-1)/(\kappa+1)\) and \(\eta=\|W-I\|\). The sufficient orbit-wide condition is \(r+(1+r)\eta<0.02097348\). With no unitary error it permits \(\kappa<1.04284557\); anisotropic gain rapidly consumes the rotation budget. This is sufficient rather than sharp. See [the nonunitary polar error budget](../0800-0899/806_a_polar_decomposition_gives_a_joint_condition_number_and_unitary_error_budget_for_nonunitary_source_map_orientation_stability.md).

That global condition-number restriction is now demoted to a last-resort bound. Because every source image is normalized separately, arbitrary positive gain on each of the four source rays is exactly invisible: a diagonal gain example with condition number \(50{,}000\) leaves every normalized orientation score unchanged. A concrete map is certified exactly by the four inequalities \(\chi_f v_f^\dagger A^\dagger\Gamma_R A v_f>0\), or linearly in positive output-Gram variables by \(\operatorname{Tr}(\chi_f\Gamma_R X_f)>0\). Only relative off-ray leakage needs control; the orbit-wide sufficient bound is \(\rho_f<0.0411023\), while direct testing is stronger. The microscopic target is therefore not a near-isometry but a map into four correct open flux cones. See [the restricted-ray and direct quadratic certificate](../0800-0899/807_diagonal_source_gains_are_orientation_invisible_and_only_relative_off_diagonal_leakage_needs_control.md).

The obvious semidefinite search is compatible but nonselective. Positive unit-trace output Grams that sum to identity, commute with the checkpoint and occupy the four prescribed bias cells reduce to a doubly stochastic mixing matrix in the spectral basis. From either correct permutation, \(Q_f(\epsilon)=(1-\epsilon)P_{\pi(f)}+\epsilon I/4\) satisfies every strict sign for all \(0\le\epsilon<1\), although it is mixed for every \(\epsilon>0\). Thus convex feasibility can falsify added Furey covariance constraints but cannot derive primitive channels. Rank one, purity, multiplicativity or positive-inverse order isomorphism is indispensable. See [the SDP nonselection theorem](../0800-0899/808_the_natural_semidefinite_furey_bias_square_relaxation_has_a_continuous_mixed_family_and_cannot_derive_rank_one_channels.md).

Jordan-triple language supplies that missing principle without an ad hoc rank objective. For the four-source and four-projector commutative Jordan algebras, \(\{x,1,z\}=x\circ z\), so any unital triple morphism preserves products. Four nonzero primitive source images must then be four mutually orthogonal nonzero idempotents summing to the identity; in a four-atom target they are necessarily a permutation of the rank-one spectral projectors. This restores exactly the 24 assignments, after which the bias square leaves two. Approximate idempotence is quantitative: if \(Q\succeq0\), \(\operatorname{Tr}Q=1\) and \(\|Q^2-Q\|<3/16\), then \(Q\) lies near a unique rank-one spectral projector with an explicit bound. See [the Jordan-triple primitive-projector rigidity theorem](../0800-0899/809_unital_jordan_triple_preservation_forces_the_four_neutral_sources_back_to_primitive_spectral_projectors_and_quantifies_approximate_purity.md).

The source side of that theorem is conditional. Four independent Yukawa Gram probes prove rank-four tomography, not closure under a physical Jordan or OPE product. An explicit set of four independent positive rank-one idempotents has both a Jordan product and a triple product outside its span, increasing rank from four to five. The canonical \(\mathbb R^4\) presently belongs to the external source labels or central selectors of four separately packaged responses, not automatically to the composite-operator span. See [the source-rank versus Jordan-closure audit](../0800-0899/810_four_independent_yukawa_gram_probes_form_a_source_label_space_not_yet_a_physical_four_atom_jordan_quotient.md).

A plain quotient of the completed Furey matter TRO cannot repair this gap. Its decomposition \(M_{3,2}\oplus M_{2,3}\oplus M_{2,1}\oplus M_{1,2}\) has four ternary-simple components, so every ideal removes whole rectangles. The unique four-dimensional retained TRO is purely leptonic, and retaining both faces of all four Yukawa channels requires the full sixteen-dimensional matter space. The quotient branch is therefore pinned and redirected to Higgs-dressed pairing. See [the completed-matter TRO quotient no-go](../0800-0899/811_the_completed_furey_matter_tro_has_no_proper_four_channel_ternary_quotient_so_the_bridge_must_use_higgs_dressed_pairing.md).

The redirected construction has an exact first step. Transpose pairs the opposite quark and lepton rectangles, and its fixed graphs are real Jordan subtriples because \(\{X^T,Y^T,Z^T\}=\{X,Y,Z\}^T\). Their undressed center is only \(\mathbb R^2\), distinguishing quark from lepton. The Higgs vacuum selects the two weak components in each graph; Gram contraction and separate source packaging then produce four central observable selectors. Thus the credible \(\mathbb R^4\) is a broken-phase response algebra, not an intrinsic atomization of the unbroken Furey triple. See [the transpose-graph and Higgs-splitting theorem](../0800-0899/812_transpose_paired_furey_rectangles_form_a_real_jordan_subtriple_but_the_higgs_vacuum_creates_the_four_channel_split.md).

That response center is now exact and falsifiable. For \(\mathcal A=\bigoplus_fH_2(\mathbb R)_f\), the center is \(\bigoplus_f\mathbb RI_f\cong\mathbb R^4\). Its Furey-handle and Higgs-channel sign elements jointly distinguish every atom; among all 24 Jordan isomorphisms to the checkpoint spectral center, preserving the handle sign and mapping channel sign to either side orientation leaves exactly the two known maps. But a nonzero cross-channel kernel \(K_{fg}\) forces the corresponding central coefficients to agree. The surviving center dimension is exactly the number of connected components of the cross-channel mixing graph; generic connected mixing collapses it to one. See [the four-channel center and mixing-graph theorem](../0800-0899/813_the_four_higgs_dressed_response_blocks_have_an_r4_center_exactly_until_cross_channel_mixing_connects_them_and_the_two_gradings_leave_two_jordan_maps.md).

Ordinary Standard-Model symmetries do not protect that four-block center. The four scalar Yukawa probes are all color/weak/hypercharge singlets with \(B=L=B-L=0\), so every one of the six cross-channel edges is symmetry-allowed. The allowed graph is \(K_4\). This does not prove the corresponding anomalous-dimension or spectral entries are nonzero; it proves that any zero requires a stronger spurion/Furey grading, Ward identity or explicit dynamical cancellation. The exact \(\mathbb R^4\) bridge is therefore a testable special structure, not a generic consequence of gauge neutrality. See [the complete allowed mixing-graph audit](../0800-0899/814_exact_standard_model_quantum_numbers_allow_the_complete_cross_channel_mixing_graph_so_the_r4_center_is_not_symmetry_protected.md).

The full channel-spurion lattice nevertheless delays every off-diagonal edge. In the zero-Yukawa limit, independent singlet rephasings give four channel charges; a coefficient mixing \(\mathcal O_g\) into \(\mathcal O_f\) must carry \(e_f-e_g\), whose minimum compensation is \(y_f^*y_g\). All six edges therefore begin at spurion degree two. The single incidence charge \((10,6,2,1)\) is too coarse and even permits a degree-one \(e-\nu\) compensation. Power counting now feeds the relative-leakage certificate, but no loop order or coefficient is inferred. See [the rank-four spurion-lattice theorem](../0800-0899/815_the_rank_four_channel_spurion_lattice_forces_cross_response_mixing_to_start_quadratically_but_the_single_incidence_grading_is_too_coarse.md).

Exact centrality is binary, whereas physical stability is spectral. A nonzero edge destroys the exact center, but an approximate channel projector survives when its off-block coupling is small relative to a nonzero diagonal spectral gap. In the exact two-channel model the established orientation tolerance is equivalent to \(|\epsilon|/|\Delta|<0.04120675\). With zero gap, every nonzero coupling rotates the eigenvectors by \(45^\circ\), however small it is. The viable approximate branch must therefore calculate both spurion-suppressed kernels and response gaps. See [the approximate-center spectral-gap theorem](../0800-0899/816_a_connected_mixing_graph_can_still_leave_an_approximate_channel_center_only_when_off_diagonal_kernels_are_small_relative_to_spectral_gaps.md).

The two crossed source gradings do not themselves force the dangerous degeneracy. Their four joint characters are distinct, so their common commutant is exactly the four-dimensional diagonal threshold algebra with unrestricted \(m_u,m_\nu,m_d,m_e\). An additional handle flip pairs \(u\) with \(\nu\) and \(d\) with \(e\); a channel flip pairs \(u\) with \(e\) and \(\nu\) with \(d\); imposing both is transitive and forces all four thresholds equal. For a general four-channel perturbation, the sufficient uniform condition is \(\|E\|/\gamma<0.03947956\). Thus broken typed symmetry permits gap protection, whereas restoring the entire abstract square makes connected mixing maximally hazardous. See [the crossed-grading threshold and flip-degeneracy theorem](../0800-0899/817_the_crossed_furey_and_higgs_gradings_allow_four_independent_thresholds_while_restoring_both_square_flips_forces_complete_degeneracy.md).

Quadratic spurion mixing now has a coefficient-independent norm envelope. If \(|c_{fg}|\le c_{\max}\) and \(a_f=|y_f|\), then \(\|E\|\) is bounded by the minimum of the Frobenius expression \(c_{\max}\sqrt{(\sum a_f^2)^2-\sum a_f^4}\) and the maximum row sum \(c_{\max}\max_f a_f\sum_{g\ne f}a_g\). Executable four-channel examples show the same hierarchical edge matrix safely below tolerance for separated thresholds and near-maximal rotation for a near-degenerate pair. The hierarchy cannot replace the gap calculation. See [the quadratic-spurion response envelope](../0800-0899/818_quadratic_spurion_edges_admit_a_closed_operator_norm_envelope_but_the_same_hierarchy_can_pass_or_fail_only_through_the_threshold_gap.md).

The fully restored square does have an exact noncentral replacement. Responses invariant under both flips form the four-parameter convolution algebra of \(V_4\), diagonalized by the Walsh–Hadamard characters: common, handle-odd, channel-odd and product-odd. Each character line has overlap \(1/2\) with every atomic channel, so its projector lies \(\sqrt3/2\) from every atomic projector—far outside the perturbative tolerance. The symmetric mixed phase is therefore not a damaged atomic phase but a canonical relational Fourier phase. Equality of its three contrast eigenvalues requires the extra isotropy \(b=c=d\). See [the \(V_4\) relational-eigenchannel theorem](../0800-0899/819_restoring_both_square_flips_turns_the_four_channel_response_into_a_v4_convolution_algebra_with_walsh_hadamard_relational_eigenchannels.md).

The three Walsh contrasts are not yet the older three relationships. Their natural Fourier metric is positive definite, whereas the \(H_2(\mathbb R)\) determinant has signature \((1,2)\), and a positive three-plane cannot embed isometrically in the primitive \((2,3)\) module. All \(V_4\)-invariant contrast forms are diagonal, so a Lorentzian form can be installed only by choosing one positive character and relative scales. Any cyclic permutation symmetry of the three characters instead forces equal coefficients and a definite metric. Dimension three therefore does not supply the Jordan or \(Spin(2,3)\) identification. See [the contrast-metric obstruction](../0800-0899/820_the_three_v4_contrasts_match_the_dimension_but_not_the_metric_of_the_h2r_relationship_space_or_a_canonical_spin23_section.md).

A symmetry that cyclically permutes the three contrasts would enlarge \(V_4\) to \(V_4\rtimes C_3\cong A_4\), forcing \(b_H=b_C=b_{HC}\) and an exact one-plus-three spectrum. It cannot be the known central selector \(C_3\): the required cycle acts noncentrally by conjugation, while the selector phase commutes in \(C_3\times S_3\); moreover groups of order four or twelve cannot be subgroups of the selector group of order eighteen. The Walsh symmetry is presently an external response symmetry, not an intertwining of the finite background torsor. See [the \(A_4\)-extension/selector mismatch theorem](../0800-0899/821_a_cyclic_symmetry_of_the_three_walsh_contrasts_would_generate_a4_and_cannot_be_the_central_c3_of_the_order18_selector_group.md).

The relational phase also changes where the final orientation bit lives. The two atomic maps differ by channel translation; Walsh transformation converts it to phases \((+,+,-,-)\) on the four characters. Those phases cancel in every rank-one character projector, so eigenvalues, populations and diagonal positive residues cannot distinguish the orientations. An orientation-odd observable must measure coherence between an even character \(\{1,H\}\) and an odd character \(\{C,HC\}\), or retain the oriented TRO amplitude before Jordan/projector reduction. See [the Walsh-phase orientation theorem](../0800-0899/822_the_atomic_orientation_bit_becomes_a_walsh_character_phase_and_is_invisible_to_relational_projector_populations.md).

The paired Furey graph can retain that phase. For a coherent Walsh amplitude \(b\), the full positive Gram \(G=bb^\dagger\) transforms as \(G\mapsto DGD\): populations and same-parity blocks remain fixed, while even–odd coherence blocks reverse sign. Only separate-channel packaging or parity-block dephasing erases the information. Real transpose pairing therefore does not itself destroy orientation; a common cross-channel phase convention is the missing physical input. See [the full-Gram coherence theorem](../0800-0899/823_the_paired_furey_graph_retains_the_walsh_orientation_signs_until_separate_channel_gram_packaging_erases_cross_character_coherence.md).

An operational relational witness is now explicit. Restrict to one even and one odd character with positive coherence matrix \(\begin{psmallmatrix}p&c\\c^*&q\end{psmallmatrix}\). Balanced linear and quadrature analyzers measure \(2\operatorname{Re}c\) and \(2\operatorname{Im}c\); both reverse under orientation while total intensity and population imbalance remain fixed. Positivity bounds the visibility \(2|c|/(p+q)\le1\). The current diagonal residue model lacks the needed coherent input and analyzer, so this is a precise experimental target rather than a claimed checkpoint prediction. See [the positive even–odd character interferometer](../0800-0899/824_a_positive_even_odd_character_interferometer_measures_relational_orientation_by_balanced_output_differences.md).

The analytic gates do not force that interferometer to dephase. The explicit reciprocal rational-inner family \(S_+(z)=U(\theta)\operatorname{diag}(b_{\gamma_1}(z)^2,b_{\gamma_2}(z)^2)U(\theta)^T\), with \(b_\gamma(z)=(z-i\gamma)/(z+i\gamma)\), is strictly Schur in the upper half-plane, unitary on the real axis and compatible with real-time sideband reality. Its Cayley transform has positive matrix pole residue \(R=U\operatorname{diag}(\gamma_1/2,\gamma_2/2)U^T\). Orientation conjugation by \(D=\operatorname{diag}(1,-1)\) reverses the off-diagonal coherence and balanced witness without changing positivity or residue eigenvalues. This proves analytic existence only: \(\theta,\gamma_1,\gamma_2\), the Furey port identification and compatibility with the full four-channel checkpoint remain open. See [the reciprocal rational-inner orientation witness](../0800-0899/825_a_reciprocal_rational_inner_two_character_response_carries_orientation_odd_coherence_and_a_positive_matrix_spectral_residue.md).

The four-channel compatibility test closes the naive independent-pair ansatz but opens a stronger coupled branch. After the spectral lines are typed by the established Furey-to-projector bias-square map—and only then Walsh transformed—the two possible even--odd perfect matchings have off-block Frobenius norms approximately \(0.84152\) and \(1.85349\), so neither can reproduce the arithmetic checkpoint. Applying Walsh directly to raw port coordinates would be a type error. The real rank-one spectral projectors \(P_j\) nevertheless give an exact response \(S(z)=\sum_j b_{\gamma_j}(z)^2P_j\), where positive \(\gamma_j\) are fitted to the four checkpoint eigenphases. It recovers \(S_0\) to numerical precision, passes reciprocity, passivity and sideband reality, and has positive residue \(R=\sum_j(\gamma_j/2)P_j\) whose typed character representation has orientation-odd even--odd blocks. The result is exact interpolation, not a threshold prediction. See [the coupled four-character checkpoint embedding](../0800-0899/826_the_arithmetic_checkpoint_rejects_two_independent_character_interferometers_but_has_an_exact_coupled_four_character_positive_residue_embedding.md).

Across the full selector orbit, the discrete eigenbundle survives but a common scalar spectral law does not. Only two of eighteen backgrounds are isospectral to the base checkpoint, so the remaining sixteen cannot arise by unitary similarity with four fixed scalar factors. Exact \(+1\) eigenvalues also require enlarging the scalar family to \(f_{\sigma,\gamma}=\sigma b_\gamma^2\): the minus branch remains Schur/inner and its Cayley function has positive atoms at \(\pm\gamma\). Finite fiberwise fits exist everywhere, but their signs realize four patterns and their scales vary by factors near three. The abstract Walsh parity remains fixed on the globally labelled bundle while its port realization moves with the spectral frame. The surviving high-value branch is a derived Möbius/Redheffer or frequency transport law, not background-by-background refitting disguised as covariance. See [the eighteen-background common-law no-go](../0800-0899/827_the_18_background_bias_labels_form_a_global_eigenbundle_but_not_a_common_four_scalar_rational_inner_law.md).

That surviving fractional branch now has an exact discrete solution. Transfer/scattering inversion turns Bogoliubov conjugation into \(\Phi_g(S)=\operatorname{Scat}(G_g\operatorname{Trans}(S)G_g^{-1})\). It reproduces all eighteen arithmetic checkpoints from \(S_0\) with maximum numerical error \(6.76\times10^{-16}\), preserves checkpoint reciprocity and unitarity, and satisfies the selector composition law. The action is genuinely nonlinear—the largest tested midpoint-affinity failure is \(0.272354\)—so it can change spectra without violating carrier covariance. Extending \(\Phi_g\) to the full analytic response is now the live gate: transformed denominators could introduce upper-half-plane poles, and finite contractivity probes do not prove global Schur or Herglotz preservation.
